#### Abstracts
##### **Selective Growing Q-Networks (SGQN): An Analysis of Adaptive Action Grids in Decoupled Q-Learning**
2610.20463v1 by Parham Nooranbakht, Seyed Mahdi Basiri Azad, Joschka Bödecker

Value-based Q-learning for continuous control can discretise the action space, but the resolution of the discretisation trades early exploration against final precision. Growing Q-Networks (GQN) grow the resolution uniformly from coarse to fine. We introduce Selective Growing Q-Networks (SGQN), which keeps decoupled Q-learning and replaces uniform growth with an adaptive action grid: bins are added next to bins with high estimated Q-value, bins with low Q-value are pruned, and actions are chosen epsilon-greedily. On eight DeepMind Control Suite (DMControl) tasks with 2 to 12 action dimensions, SGQN has the highest mean final return among the compared methods on four, is within 1.1% of the best method on three more, and is 3.4% below Bang-Bang MPO on Walker Run, using on average 7.5 of 9 available bins per dimension. We then test whether the adaptive grid explains these results, and find that it does not. Growth activates every candidate bin and behaves like uniform growth, and removing pruning changes the return by at most 1.1%. Two other choices matter: starting from a coarse grid, since starting with all 9 bins produces collapsed seeds, and epsilon-greedy selection, since an annealed softmax lowers the return by 23% to 100%. SGQN is insensitive to its growth and pruning hyperparameters. It also differs from the GQN and DecQN baselines in other respects, notably one gradient update per environment step instead of one per eight, which we did not control for, so we do not attribute its advantage to a single component. We present SGQN as a competitive value-based method and as an analysis of which ingredients of a growing action grid matter.

摘要：

##### **From Training to Escalation: Forensic Analysis and Local Recreation of RL/RLHF-Induced Privilege Escalation in AI Agents (CVE-2026-65616)**
2610.20437v1 by Alicia Colmenero-Fernández

Mid-2026 reporting on autonomous AI evaluation incidents claimed that frontier large language model (LLM) agents escaped server containment through emergent coordination and cloud infrastructure attacks. This paper contextualizes those claims through forensic analysis and containerized recreation of both the Highly Persistent Internal Model (HPIM) training-case conditions and the Phase One privilege escalation targeting internal package repositories during OpenAI's ExploitGym evaluations. We recreate the configuration of the OpenAI-provided training example and reproduce the cryptographic verification bypass (CVE-2026-65616) in isolated repository servers, where misconfigured systems enabled token forgery and privilege escalation through basic HTTP requests, Python, and JavaScript. Crucially, the training-example conditions and the Phase One (and later) misconfigurations share the same exploitable patterns. Our analysis explains how Zero Trust Architecture principles neutralize this shared attack vector. Contemporary AI risks derive directly from training and deployment conditions in ambivalent learning environments lacking adequate containment measures.

摘要：

##### **Robust Counterfactuals for Markov Decision Processes under Statistical and Causal Uncertainties**
2610.20210v1 by Jessica Lally, Milad Kazemi, Nicola Paoletti

Counterfactual reasoning (asking what would have happened, had a decision been different) is a powerful tool for identifying causes of failure and explaining the behaviour of AI systems in sequential decision-making tasks. In Markov Decision Processes (MDPs), two sources of uncertainty complicate counterfactual inference: statistical uncertainty in the MDP transitions when the MDP is estimated from trajectory data, and uncertainty about the unknown causal model, as there are multiple structural causal models (SCMs) compatible with each MDP. Existing work addresses these two challenges in isolation. We address both simultaneously by introducing a framework for partial counterfactual inference in MDPs that is robust to both statistical and causal uncertainty. Our approach provides: 1) closed-form analytical bounds for the counterfactual probabilities that are valid across all SCMs entailed by a given interval MDP (IMDP); 2) a method to compute minimal robust interventions to achieve a desired counterfactual outcome via pessimistic value iteration on the induced counterfactual IMDP. Since the latter is constructed using our analytical bounds, our approach remains efficient and scalable to realistic MDP environments. Experiments across multiple case studies demonstrate our method's robustness and practical utility for identifying minimal sets of actions that, if changed, would have led to improved outcomes.

##### **Trainable at Depth, Solvable at Small Size: What the Loss Surface of a Polynomial Activation Dictates for Designing, Initializing and Training Networks**

What makes a deep network train, and what shape is its loss surface? We answer both with one construction at two scales: a polynomial activation f (z) = z + λz 3 , clamped and projected onto the unit sphere, plus an unnormalized identity path. As a deep stack it trains to one hundred layers on MNIST with no normalization machinery; as a small model its square loss is a polynomial system we solve exactly. The small scale explains the large one: it prescribes how to design, initialize and train the stack.

##### **Learning to Play: Reinforcement Learning and Games**
2610.17391v1 by Aske Plaat

Amazing breakthroughs in reinforcement learning have taken place. Computers teach themselves to play Chess and Go and beat world champions. There is talk about expanding application areas towards general artificial intelligence (AI). The breakthroughs in Backgammon, Checkers, Chess, Atari, Go, Poker, and StarCraft have shown that we can build machines that exhibit intelligent game playing of the highest level. These successes have been widely publicized in the media, and inspire AI entrepreneurs and scientists alike. Reinforcement learning in games has become a mainstream AI research topic. It is a broad topic, and the successes build on a range of diverse techniques, from exact planning algorithms, to adaptive sampling, deep function approximation, and ingenious self-play methods. Perhaps because of the breadth of these technologies, or because of the recency of the breakthroughs, there are few books that explain these methods in depth. This book covers all methods in one comprehensive volume, explaining the latest research, bringing you as close as possible to working implementations, with many references to the original research papers. The programming examples in this book are in Python, the language in which most current reinforcement learning research is conducted. We help you to get started with machine learning frameworks such as Gym, TensorFlow, and Keras, and provide exercises to help understand how AI is learning to play.

摘要：

##### **Does a Governed Confidence Score Know When a Text-to-SQL Answer Is Right? An Exploratory Study on BIRD and a Pre-registered Confirmation on Spider**
2610.17256v1 by Raghu Venkat, Tricha Anjali

Conversational analytics lets people ask questions of records spread across many systems and get answers in plain language; it is useful only if each answer can be trusted and explained. A production system of this kind attaches a confidence score to every answer it writes over a database and uses it to show the answer, show it with a warning, or withhold it. The score's weights, caps and thresholds were set by design. This paper audits that design. On the 1,534 questions of BIRD dev, answered by GPT-5.1 with a benchmark-adapted production prompt and five samples per question, the designed score separates right from wrong answers weakly (AUROC 0.624, 95% CI 0.595-0.652) and withholds 35.7% of correct answers. Nearly all of its ranking signal comes from agreement among the samples, and most of it is lost where agreement is combined by a minimum with a checklist that drops to zero on any unsupported claim, including claims in commentary. We froze a rule in which agreement with the shown answer is the score and deterministic checks act as vetoes and caps, and pre-registered a test on Spider dev before any Spider answer existed. The primary hypothesis held: AUROC rose from 0.566 to 0.725 (paired difference +0.159, 95% CI +0.097 to +0.221), and correct answers withheld fell from 49.0% to 8.8% with PASS accuracy 95.7%. Under one matching policy the choice of agreement statistic matters little; the gain comes from how agreement enters the score. Whether added checks help depends on how correctness is defined, while they always cost unqualified coverage. A pre-registered empty-result check did not meet its effect-size criterion on Spider. Correctness here means the core answer; labels are model-judged, and a blinded two-person audit, not yet complete, will be reported in a revised version.

##### **ChemExplain: A Multimodal Benchmark for Explanatory Chemical Reasoning**
2610.17183v1 by Aotian Chen, Frazier N. Baker, Reza Averly, Shan Zhang, Tianao Chen, Daniel Adu-Ampratwum, Xia Ning, Yuan Xue

Current chemistry benchmarks primarily evaluate outcome prediction or reaction pathway generation, but rarely test whether a model can explain why an observed chemical effect occurs. We introduce ChemExplain, a benchmark of 1,180 multiple-choice chemistry questions drawn from 383 peer-reviewed papers. Each question pairs a molecular or reaction image with an observed chemical effect and four candidate explanations, each associated with a different structural feature visible in the image. Answering requires the model to perceive the depicted structure, apply chemical knowledge, and identify which feature most directly explains the effect, a capability we term explanatory attribution. The correct feature-mechanism relation is derived from supporting evidence in the source paper rather than invented by the question-generation LLM. We construct the benchmark automatically with a multi-agent framework that applies progressive quality control at three stages and validate it with eight chemistry domain experts. The strongest evaluated model reaches 78.6% accuracy, leaving considerable room for improvement. On a 600-question expert-reviewed subset, reasoning-trace analysis classifies 71.4% of the strongest model's errors as attribution failures. Attribution is also the largest error category for the open-source and chemistry-specific models included in our analysis.

摘要：

##### **Audit tools of the trade: Automated Auditor Elicitation Can Be Sensitive to the Context the Auditor Is Given**
2610.16911v1 by Ben Slater, Ryan Lundqvist, Leo Richter, Kevin Wei

Does providing additional context to automated AI auditors improve their effectiveness? Using the Petri tool to audit LLM coding agents, we test the impact of providing two types of resources (an external codebase and benchmark transcripts) to LLM auditors, across four auditor-target LLM pairs and three behaviours (harmful compliance, reward hacking, sycophancy). (1) External codebase: We provide the auditor LLM with a fixed, external open source codebase, which prior work finds improves the realism of synthetic audits. Providing this resource suppresses reward hacking in both auditor-target pairs whose baseline rate is above the floor (4.8 to 1.3 and 3.9 to 1.8 on Petri's default 1-10 scale); the remaining two pairs sit at the floor throughout. The suppression recurs in three of four pairs under a different scenario and resource bundle. (2) External benchmark transcripts: We provide the auditor with transcripts of the target's performance on benchmarks measuring similar behaviours, intended to inform the auditor about the target's behavioural patterns. Providing this resource raises reward hacking scores in all four pairs (from 1.7 to 6.1 in one case). However, subsequent experiments show that supplying only the related benchmark's abstract, without transcripts, also increases reward hacking, and the effect is absent under a different framing of the same behaviour. Overall, we find no consistent effect of either resource outside reward hacking, but we find substantial variability in judge-scored behaviour across conditions. Our results underscore the importance of careful piloting with tools such as Petri, as the measured rate of target behaviour is highly sensitive to configuration decisions. We hope our results inspire more research into the reliability and robustness of automated alignment auditing.

摘要：

##### **Integrating Feature Understandability into Feature Selection for Human-Centred Tabular Explanations**
2610.16627v1 by Nicola Rossberg, Bennett Kleinberg, Barry O'Sullivan, Luca Longo, Andrea Visentin

Existing human-centred XAI research predominantly evaluates explanations after they have been generated. However, this fails to provide actionable insights into improving accessibility. This study incorporates human-based feature understandability into feature selection to improve the generation of human-centred explanations. Natural language explanations for machine learning models trained on tabular data often cite influential features in the input data to explain a given decision. As a consequence, users' ability to understand the produced explanation hinges on their understanding of the cited features. To quantify this natural-language feature understandability, Rossberg et al. proposed the Feature Understandability Scale, which assigns numerical `understandability scores' to features based on user feedback. To evaluate the degree to which these scores can improve machine learning explanation quality, this study collects understandability scores across two datasets, proposes an understandability-aware feature selection algorithm to select features that are both understandable and informative, and trains a random forest based on these feature subsets. The SHAP-based explanations generated by this model are evaluated using user feedback, and changes in explanation quality are assessed. The individual evaluation of the explanations showed that while the understandability-based explanations scored higher on average, the difference was not statistically significant. In contrast, the comparative evaluation found that participants preferred these explanations significantly more frequently than expected at the chance level. It is concluded that the integration of understandability can yield more understandable explanations at minimal cost to classification performance, although changes to quality require elucidation in larger cohorts.

摘要：

##### **AI Discovery in Combinatorics: From Problems to Solutions and Back to Bigger Problems**
2610.16555v1 by Eugenio Cainelli, Lorenzo Luccioli, Alessandro Iraci, Giovanni Paolini

We introduce $qt$Bench, a benchmark of 29 open problems in algebraic combinatorics. These problems ask for a simple combinatorial algorithm that explains given polynomial coefficients or identities. To help AI agents search for such algorithms, we provide a scaffold for playing with combinatorial objects, countering cheating attempts, and testing candidate solutions. To explore how far frontier AI systems can go on these problems, we collaborated with OpenAI, whose researchers obtained proposed solutions to several of them. This highlights bigger meta-problems: is AI discovery at scale ethical? How should these results be released? How should proof digestion be organized? We discuss these questions and give our views.

摘要：

##### **pi-Net: Empirical Latent Representations for Differentiable Physics Heads in Data-Scarce Systems**
2610.16504v1 by Akshay Dhalpe, Arnab Chattopadhyay, Shahriar Bakrani Balani, Eric Cortanéa, Sasu Tarkoma

Developing reliable predictive models for multi-physics systems often forces a compromise between computationally demanding physical simulators and data-hungry, opaque machine learning (ML) algorithms. While incorporating dimensional analysis into directed computation graphs offers a structurally sound alternative to explicitly solving partial differential equations (PDEs), such formulations typically yield underdetermined systems that act as imperfect simulators. We introduce pi-Net, a physics-informed latent variable model that resolves these imperfect simulators. The hybrid pi-Net architecture consists of an encoder that maps inputs into physically normalized latent space, which are then decoded into final predictions by a differentiable physics head. Evaluated on a real-world, data-scarce Gas Metal Arc Welding (GMAW) case study, the framework achieves an R2 of 0.864 with only 60 experimental samples. The results demonstrate that pi-Net significantly outperforms purely data-driven baselines in data-scarce regimes while providing causal, process-level explainability.

摘要：

##### **XAI-Guided Fourier Domain Generalisation for Pure-Simulation Sim-to-Real Deep Reinforcement Learning**
2610.16478v1 by Wei Yao, Chuyao Wang, Ziwei Wang, Nabil Aouf

Deep reinforcement learning has shown strong potential for vision-based autonomous driving, but policies trained in simulation often suffer severe performance degradation when deployed in the real world. Existing sim-to-real methods commonly address this gap through image-transfer-based domain adaptation or policy-agnostic domain randomisation. However, the former requires laborious acquisition of target-domain data and is often limited to the deployment domain, whereas the latter introduces unguided perturbations that may not reflect the underlying sim-to-real discrepancy, leading to suboptimal transfer performance. To overcome these limitations, we propose XFD, an explainable artificial intelligence (XAI)-guided Fourier domain generalisation framework for pure-simulation sim-to-real reinforcement learning. The proposed framework extracts explainability attribution maps from simulated observations, and converts these attributions into policy-conditioned Fourier-domain interventions. By perturbing appearance-sensitive spectral components while preserving task-defining spatial structures, XFD constructs robustness-enhancing visual domains entirely within simulation. Subsequently, the proposed robust policy optimisation guides the policy towards attending to task-critical features while suppressing sensitivity to irrelevant visual information. Extensive experiments in simulation and zero-shot real-world deployment demonstrate that XFD consistently improves traveled distance and reward over standard policy and randomly domain-randomised baseline, especially on unseen maps with cluttered backgrounds. Attribution-based analysis further proves that the proposed policy preserves more consistent task-relevant visual evidence across simulation and real-world observations.

##### **VisExMEM: Visual Evidence-Based Explainable Multimodal Evaluation Metric**
2610.16441v1 by Subhadeep Roy, Christoph Leiter, Steffen Eger

Current image-text evaluation often relies on a global score, which gives limited insight into which semantic details are supported by the image. Explainable metrics provide rationales, but the visual evidence supporting those rationales can still be difficult to verify. We introduce VisExMEM, a reference-free evaluator that decomposes captions into atomic claims, localizes visual evidence, verifies each claim, and averages claim-level support into a global score. Explanations use the same evidence and decisions. We train Qwen3.5-based 4B and 9B models on 100K samples with multi-task SFT, counterfactual refinement, and evidence-aware GDPO. Across eight benchmarks, VisExMEM-9B achieves the highest descriptive composite among open-source evaluators in our comparison, exceeding VQAScore by 11.7 points while remaining competitive with GPT-5.6 Sol. We also introduce AGITA-Bench, containing 600 human-annotated image-text pairs and 8.7K atomic claims for global and claim-level evaluation. For untuned Qwen3.5 and GPT-5.6 Sol controls, decomposed prompting raises the same composite by 3.6-5.1 points relative to holistic prompting. Human evaluation shows that VisExMEM explanations outperform prior explainable metrics and approach GPT-5.6 Sol.

##### **GUIDE: Computationally Augmented Consensus for Trustworthy Clinical Practice Guidelines**
2610.16108v1 by Yuanfan Lin, Jie Di, Xujin He, Zhenzhe Lin, Jialing Chen, Ruiyang Li, Changyu Zheng, Yuanrui Shang, Weiqin Peng, Jingxi Qin, Xiong Wang, Dongxin Ye, Yining Huang, Guoqiang Li, Andi Xu, Sijia Yu, Ruixin Wang, Yahan Yang, Xiaohang Wu, Duoru Lin, Jingjing Chen, Zhenzhen Liu, Wenben Chen, Haotian Lin

Maintaining up-to-date clinical practice guidelines (CPGs) is essential for evidence-based medicine, but rapid evidence growth and complex expert consensus formation limit timely updates. We developed GUIDE, a multi-agent copilot system for human-AI collaborative guideline generation and updating. GUIDE delegates labor-intensive tasks to specialized AI agents while preserving expert judgment and final arbitration. In 12 NICE ophthalmology datasets, GUIDE achieved 88.85% literature recall, 86.06% alignment in certainty-of-evidence ratings, and 91.67% recommendation agreement, requiring 162.17 minutes per dataset on average (28.68% human intervention). Performance remained stable across 20 additional datasets from cardiology, endocrinology, gastroenterology, and respiratory medicine. Ablation analysis showed that removing expert intervention reduced performance in judgment-intensive steps such as Risk-of-Bias assessment. In six post-cutoff datasets, GUIDE maintained robust end-to-end performance on unseen evidence. Prospective real-world validation showed that GUIDE reduced expert workload and produced more clinically acceptable recommendations than the original decade-old guideline (mean score, 3.73 vs. 1.53; agreement, 66.7% vs. 13.3%), with outcomes comparable to human-only updating (P = 0.588) but substantially less time and effort. GUIDE offers a practical framework for timely, trustworthy, expert-centric guideline development and validates computationally augmented consensus.

##### **Where Polanyi Meets Galois: Tacit Knowing as an Algebro-Deterministic Operation, Toward Re-rooting AI-Generated Science**
2610.16102v1 by Hiroyuki Chuma, Kanji Otsuka, Yoichi Sato, Kodo Yamauchi

We argue that tacit knowing is better modelled as an operating regime of one memory substrate than as a kind of content. The substrate is VaCoAl, an algebro-deterministic XOR-and-shift hyperdimensional memory over GF(2), realized as CASRAM (commercial SRAM-CAM) and PyVaCoAl (DRAM-CAM).In VaCoAl/PyVaCoAl a directed acyclic graph (DAG) reads two ways. Repairing every collision (Rescue mode) makes the trace exactly auditable but ranks no path higher; tolerating collisions (Don't Care mode) yields a path-integrated confidence, CR$_2$, that silently favours direct routes. This audit-valuation complementarity is a formal counterpart of Polanyi's claim that tacit and explicit knowledge are not sharply divided. It separates contingent tacitness, which a new query brings out (AlphaGo's game tree), from regime-bound tacitness, statable only in a regime where it is gone (AlphaGo's hunches). (1) Polanyi's from-to structure reads as non-commutative binding, the Identikit as role-filler composition, recognition as majority-vote coincidence. (2) Explicating the valuation destroys it, as attending to his fingers can make Polanyi's pianist falter, yet a closed-form law states it. Polanyi, Nonaka, Tsoukas and Collins each capture part of this but narrowly; even Nonaka and von Krogh's continuum cannot tell contingent from regime-bound tacitness. (3) Collins's three tacit knowledges become strata, one outside the substrate. (4) Our mentor-student DAG is a tacit-transmission graph. (5) Terence Tao's remark that AI-solved problems spawn few follow-ups is diagnosed as rootless explicit knowledge: checkable results whose route no one can follow and that no one yet understands well enough to extend, teach or judge. To re-root them, LLMs generate, VaCoAl/PyVaCoAl keeps an auditable trace with its confidence, and people value, a role virtue epistemology shows cannot be offloaded. Testable predictions follow.

##### **The Sharp Directions Are Against You: Curvature Analysis of Activation Steering**
2610.16084v1 by Seyedarmin Azizi, Arya Fayyazi, Parsa Razmara, Erfan Baghaei Potraghloo, Souvik Kundu, Massoud Pedram

Activation steering adds a vector to a language model's internal representations at inference time. It is a lightweight alternative to fine-tuning for behavioral control. The standard construction, contrastive activation addition (CAA), is fragile, as on many inputs it shifts behavior in the wrong direction. To explain this fragility, we first study the geometry of the steering vector. We introduce steering Hessian, the matrix of second order derivative of the model's behavioral loss with respect to the steering vector. It captures the output behavior response sensitivity of the model under small perturbations of the steering vector along each direction. Its top eigenvectors are sharp directions: tiny perturbations along them can cause large, erratic, input-dependent behavioral swings. Bottom eigenvectors are flat directions: behavior is robust along them. Removing the sharp component of a steering vector approximately preserves its direction and magnitude (cosine > 0.974, norm reduction < 3% for typical settings) while reducing the vector's directional curvature by up to 12x (median 4x across 36 conditions). We evaluate on three model families (Qwen2.5-7B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B), with four behavioral tasks, and 36 token-level conditions. Steering along sharp directions reverses the intended behavior in every condition tested, with anti-steer rates of 53-100%. Sharp directions account for only 1-5% of the CAA vector's squared norm. However, they drive a disproportionate share of the gap between the steered and unsteered next-token distributions during autoregressive generation. We term this one-line correction as sharp removal; it outperforms standard CAA in open-ended generation across all 25 conditions tested. A jailbreaking experiment further shows that sharp directions act as noise rather than signal: they disrupt coherent behavioral control regardless of steering polarity.

##### **AI Representations of Mental Health Stigma: Quantifying the Gap Between AI Estimations and Human Perceptions Across Gender Groups Using GPT-Based LLMs**
2610.16042v1 by Soojong Kim, Hye Min Kim, Kwanho Kim, Dongdong Zhu

The increasing use of artificial intelligence (AI) in mental health applications raises concerns about its potential to internalize and perpetuate societal biases. This research evaluates both disparities and misalignment in how large language models (LLMs), GPT-4 and GPT-5, represent perceptions of mental health across gender groups. The research comprises two studies. Study 1 applied widely adopted measures of Social Distance, Stereotypes, and Attitudes Toward Seeking Help to assess whether the AI models depict systematic gender differences. Study 2 compared AI estimations of Personal, Self, and Perceived Stigma with large-scale real-world human data from the Healthy Minds Study (N = 76,406) to evaluate alignment between AI estimations and human perceptions. Results from Study 1 showed the AI models consistently portrayed men as having the greatest desire for social distance from someone with mental health problems, relatively higher Stereotypes scores, and the least favorable attitudes toward help-seeking, compared with women and nonbinary individuals. Study 2 confirmed that this ordinal pattern partially aligned with human-reported data for Personal and Self Stigma. However, the AI models systematically underestimated Personal Stigma and Perceived Stigma while substantially overestimating Self Stigma for all gender groups. These findings reveal that, although the AI models have learned relative gender-based patterns reflecting human experiences to a certain extent, they function as distorted portrayals, highlighting the need for rigorous, context-specific validation against real-world data to ensure AI's safe and equitable deployment.

##### **Hardware-Assisted Multi-Stage Ransomware Detection with Adaptive Machine Learning**
2610.15951v1 by Salma Aboelmagd, Md. Rakibul Ahasan, Zhixin Pan, Abdulrahman Takiddin, Ziyu Shu

Ransomware is a fast-escalating privacy threat causing significant financial and data losses worldwide. As attacks grow more sophisticated, traditional static signatures and dynamic behavior analysis struggle to defend. In this paper, we propose an novel framework that couples software-level scanning with hardware-level microprocessor activity monitoring to achieve efficient ransomware detection and mitigation. The contributions are threefold: (1) we employ adversarial training to harden static analysis against obfuscation and leverage hardware-assisted monitoring for real-time detection; (2) we integrate neural architecture search (NAS) to automatically tailor models for stronger generalizability; and (3) We validate the proposed approach through extensive experiments, demonstrating substantially improved detection accuracy and reduced latency compared with existing methods. Moreover, it provides trace-level interpretability that explains the underlying decision process.

##### **Multi-Horizon Explainable Queue Forecasting and Resource-Aware Decision Support for Intelligent Border Control Using LightGBM**
2610.15930v1 by Varvara Mama, Fani D. Christidi, Nikolaos C. Kapsalis, Anargyros T. Baklezos, Christos D. Nikolopoulos

Border crossing points are stochastic service systems in which traffic demand, traveler categories, registration status, inspection requirements, staffing, and lane availability interact on multiple time scales. This paper extends an earlier multimodal queue-management framework by introducing an implementation-oriented, multi-horizon forecasting and decision-support architecture based on Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LightGBM) models. In contrast to a single forecasting block, the proposed approach constructs horizon-specific regressors for 15, 30, 60, and 120 min forecasts and jointly estimates future waiting time and queue lengths. The models operate on temporally lagged traffic, service, density, and queue features, while an operational layer incorporates lane compatibility, officer availability, possibly degraded resources, traveler category, preregistration information, and human-approval constraints. Quantile LightGBM models provide 95\% prediction intervals for waiting-time forecasts. Beyond prediction, the framework produces machine-readable recommendations, alerts, aggregate key performance indicators, confidence information, and integration outputs suitable for border resource-management and decision-support systems. The results establish a reproducible synthetic-data baseline and a practical interface for subsequent validation with operational border-crossing data.

##### **RetryBench: Separating Retry Permission from Recovery Outcomes in GPT Tool Use**
2610.15884v1 by You Liu, Yue Liu, Quanchao Lu

False retry permission (FRP) occurs when an agent retries a timed-out tool call without support from the visible contract. RetryBench separates this error from realized recovery. Successful protected recovery does not imply reliable rejection of unsupported retries. In a 720-response key control, valid keys usually enabled safe progress, but offered changed keys elicited unsupported retries in Sol (1/80), Terra (55/80), and Luna (60/80). A later 240-response control removed the candidate and required models to supply a key. Luna still made 8/80 unsupported dispatches; Sol and Terra made none in 80 responses each. Accepting a displayed bad key therefore cannot explain all observed errors, although changed output requirements and conditions preclude a pure display-effect comparison. Removing the weak-contract label increased retries in Sol and Terra's coded-action conditions in a six-endpoint study. A separate 1,536-response canonical-action block also found a positive Terra contrast (115/128 versus 124/128 retries), concentrated in two task/order conditions; the other five contrasts remained unresolved after multiplicity correction. An explicit-contract cue control elicited deferral in all 762 returned responses, leaving recovery unresolved. These results support reporting permission and recovery together across declared interface variants. The evidence covers four purposive tasks, fixed GPT endpoints, and one recovery decision; it identifies neither internal mechanisms nor episode-level safety.

##### **An Explainable System for Offensive Content Detection in Nastaliq Urdu: Corpus Curation, Transformer Benchmarking, and Word-Level Attribution**
2610.15795v1 by Muhammad Shahid Iqbal Malik

Automated moderation systems are deployed at scale, yet they remain opaque to the users and moderators affected by their decisions, and their coverage is almost entirely confined to English. For Urdu written in the Nastaliq script, no such system exists, and the annotated resources it would be built on are drawn largely from Twitter/X, where character limits constrain how users write. Interpretability has received little attention, leaving it unclear what such models actually learn. This study addresses these gaps in three steps. First, we curate and publicly release a corpus of 7604 Nastaliq Urdu Facebook comments, extending an earlier corpus by correcting text-level inconsistencies and re-examining the original labels. Second, we fine-tune four Urdu-capable transformers under a common grid-search protocol and compare them against twelve feature-classifier baselines on identical data partitions. The best configuration, Multilingual Representations for Indian Languages (MuRIL), reaches 87.05% accuracy and 87.06% macro F1, improving on the strongest baseline by 5.52 percentage points, a margin that exceeds the 95% confidence interval on test accuracy; the two leading transformers differ by less than the test set can resolve, and performance is stable across five optimizers. Third, we compute word-level SHAP attributions adapted to right-to-left rendering. Attribution mass concentrates on high-frequency slurs but extends to syntactically neutral function words and to political vocabulary characteristic of the source pages, indicating that aggregate performance may rest partly on lexical and topical regularities rather than a generalizable representation of offensiveness.

##### **Siam-ALS: An Explainable Siamese Deep Learning Framework for Early ALS Detection Using EEG and Eye-Tracking Signals**
2610.15351v1 by Abdur Rahman, A. A. M. Muzahid, Istiak Ahmed, Dipto Biswas, Hua Han, Yujin Zhang, Ferdous Sohel

Amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) diagnosis remains challenging due to heterogeneous symptoms and reliance on clinical assessment, underscoring the need for objective, data-driven approaches that leverage patient-generated physiological signals. We propose Siam-ALS, an explainable Siamese deep learning framework that jointly models synchronized electroencephalography (EEG) and eye-tracking (ET) signals. EEG captures ALS-related changes in brain activity, while ET provides information about oculomotor control, allowing the model to learn complementary patterns associated with ALS. The framework employs two modality-specific encoders: a convolutional EEG encoder that captures spatiotemporal neural patterns and an MLP-based ET encoder that models oculomotor dynamics. These modality-specific features are processed through weight-sharing Siamese branches and fused into a compact 64-dimensional joint embedding. A lightweight classifier is used to predict ALS, while pairwise metric learning helps the model learn more effective patterns from the small, diverse ALS datasets. Performance was evaluated on the public EEGET-ALS dataset using strict subject-level cross-validation, where Siam-ALS achieved an accuracy of 0.988 and an AUROC of 0.998, demonstrating strong performance compared with classical machine learning, DL, and non-Siamese fusion approaches. Cross-dataset evaluation on the independent BigP3BCI dataset achieved an AUROC of approximately 0.98, supporting the generalization capability of Siam-ALS across different BCI paradigms. To enhance interpretability, six XAI methods were evaluated in the learned embedding space, with SHAP achieving the highest, though statistically comparable, fidelity in identifying important EEG and ET patterns that contribute to ALS predictions. Overall, Siam-ALS provides an accurate, generalizable, and explainable framework for multimodal ALS detection.

##### **ClinEdge: An Expert-Graded Benchmark of Real Patient Edge Cases Where Frontier Multimodal Models Fail**
2610.15239v1 by Anurag Sharma, Aditya Sharma, Sapna Baalwan

Most benchmarks for large language models in medicine are built from licensing examinations and theoretical questions. The few that draw on real clinical data do so only in part, and rarely require a model to read a patient's own imaging. ClinEdge addresses this gap with 32 real, de-identified patient cases from active clinical practice, each evaluated across seven frontier systems for a total of 224 expert-graded evaluations. Each case pairs primary imaging with a short vignette, contains a feature known to defeat pattern-matched reasoning, and is scored against the patient's documented clinical course. The suite is built for depth rather than volume. Grading followed a two-stage expert process. First, a senior orthopaedic surgeon graded all 224 responses on a weighted rubric and a pass-or-fail verdict. Second, a panel of nineteen physicians, largely senior specialists at teaching medical colleges, independently reviewed every case and every one of the 224 grades. All seven production systems were evaluated through their public interfaces, and all 224 conversations are preserved as replayable links.
  Performance was poor across all systems, and the failures were shared: different vendors missed the same findings and converged on the same wrong plans. From the experts' notes we catalogue 18 recurring failure modes in five families. The models stop reading an image at the first finding. They give generic plans and lean toward over-treatment. They trust a misleading history over correct imaging. Their answers vary across systems and runs, and change with question format. Failing answers were delivered fluently and without any expressed uncertainty. Four cases are released in full; the remaining 28 are held out, and we invite frontier AI labs and healthcare AI companies to evaluate their models against them (research@futrbridge.com)

##### **TurnCost: Turns, Not Observations: Cache-Aware Loop Engineering for Coding Agents**
2610.15093v1 by Pramod Kumar, Raj Kumar Pandey

AI coding agents operate in iterative loops: they modify code, run tools, inspect the results, and decide what to do next. We call the design of this process loop engineering, which includes context management, model selection, compaction, and termination. Existing efficiency work mainly compresses tool observations or routes selected turns to cheaper models, but the practical opportunity for these optimizations remains unclear. We analyze 600 publicly released coding-agent trajectories spanning two agent scaffolds and two language models. Median observations contain only 118-286 tokens, while the large build logs commonly used to motivate compression occur near the ninety-ninth percentile. Moreover, 98.6% of input tokens are served from a prefix cache, so flat input pricing overestimates loop cost by roughly 7x on average under near-ideal caching.

We release TurnCost, a tool for re-scoring trajectory corpora under alternative caching, pricing, compression, and routing policies.

##### **Iterate on an LLM, Serve on an SLM**
2610.14576v1 by Keegan Wang

As frontier LLMs gain more capabilities, the cost to run them also goes up. At the same time, small language models (SLMs) also gain more capabilities but have largely been able to keep their cost stable, offering 5-40 times cheaper inference for the narrow, repetitive subtasks that agents decompose work into. ##### **Reels-RSI: Program-Level Self-Improvement for Code-Rendered Explainer Videos**
2610.14495v1 by Wei Lin

Explainer videos -- a worked geometry problem, why the sky is blue, how a dynasty fell -- are usually one more target for pixel video generation. A program can be read, diffed, tested and audited; pixels can only be watched. Reels-RSI is an open-source reel director for any agent harness; its self-improvement happens at the program level rather than in weights or prompts. Every revision pass is logged with a code snapshot; a retrospective pairs each check finding with the diff that fixed it and proposes lessons; a lesson that a static check can catch is compiled into a rule with a must-fail and a must-pass example, so the memory is unit-tested; and a rule learned today runs over everything made before it. A bounded, benchmark-gated step lets an agent rewrite the director's playbook, kept only when blind pairwise judgments on held-out topics prefer it; nothing enters memory without a human decision. Across 111 reels -- a field study, two playbook evolutions, a 2x2 memory ablation, a weaker director, a 48-reel learning curve and a judge-reliability study -- we find that the rule learned in the field caught four latent defects in frames authored before it existed and none after; that rules are an audit for a strong director (eight unguarded reels, no violation) and a guard for a weaker one (thirty violations unguarded versus five); that lessons shorten revision loops but do not compound or move the judge; and that the first rewrite's 11--1 verdict was a position bias in our vote design (judges pick the second-shown reel 62--81% of the time), masking scripts that traded thirteen facts for craft. We document four failure modes of the preference signal, which check caught each, and the fixes. Code, rules, lessons and data are public.

This article offers an overview of recent developments in deep learning from a control vantage point. ##### **The RNN Homunculus: Learning to Gate Long-Term Memory**

Learning long-range dependencies with recurrent neural networks (RNNs) is difficult because of the vanishing gradient problem. We study this gate-learning problem through carefully controlled sequence classification tasks. Motivated by this observation, we find that distilling the input Jacobians of short-horizon policies into long-horizon policies yields significant performance gains, especially in combinatorial manipulation tasks. Taken together, our results highlight goal relabeling horizon as an important consideration when learning generalist policies from offline data.

摘要：學習目標達成策略的困難通常歸因於一種「地平線詛咒」，這種詛咒表現為時間差備份中的偏差累積和噪聲優勢估計。在這項工作中，我們識別出在目標條件策略學習中存在一種額外的信息性地平線詛咒，增加目標重新標記的地平線會顯著降低策略的泛化能力和性能。通過一系列與預言規劃者的受控實驗，我們將訓練期間採樣的目標地平線與在測試時要求策略達成的目標地平線解耦。即使僅在一系列相近的子目標上進行評估，目標條件行為克隆（BC）策略也會遭受嚴重的、依賴於訓練地平線的性能下降，而這種下降是可以通過強化學習（RL）目標來緩解的。我們將這一現象解釋為動作與事後重新標記的目標之間的條件互信息隨地平線的變化而減少，並實證發現，無論是BC還是RL策略，在長地平線目標上訓練時，其敏感性從目標信息轉向狀態信息，這一點可以通過策略的輸入雅可比矩陣來衡量。受到這一觀察的啟發，我們發現將短地平線策略的輸入雅可比矩陣提煉到長地平線策略中會產生顯著的性能提升，特別是在組合操作任務中。綜合來看，我們的結果突顯了在從離線數據學習通用策略時，目標重新標記地平線是一個重要的考量。

##### **CurveTQ: Rotation-Free Trellis Quantization of LLM Weights via Curvature-Weighted Search**
2610.09212v1 by Guanhua Ding, Zi Wang, Ruichao Li, Jack Liu

The best two-bit weight quantizers for large language models, such as QTIP and Proteus, rotate each weight matrix by a random orthogonal transform, which must be undone at every decoding step, then encode it with a trellis or lattice code under a Euclidean search; the layer Hessian enters only through error feedback between coding blocks. We show that this leaves part of the Hessian unused. Error feedback turns the loss into a weighted sum of per-coordinate rounding errors whose weights, the diagonal of the Hessian's LDL factorization, existing quantizers compute but never read. We put these weights into the Viterbi branch metric, so the search follows the curvature within each coding block. This also explains the rotation: it removes this within-block variation, so weighting in the native basis and rotating are substitutes. On three models the weighted native search matches a full-dimension randomized Hadamard to within about one point of downstream accuracy, and weighting after the rotation gains little. Around this search we build CurveTQ, a trellis codec with no rotation, which handles the weights' amplitude and marginal shape with a factored scale field and a closed-form quantile table, and stores a start state per coding block so the trellis can adapt to the residual that error feedback carries into it. At two bits CurveTQ is 1-3 points higher in mean downstream accuracy than QTIP and Proteus on three 4-8B Instruct models, even after both are given our start state, which alone lifts either baseline by 1-3 points. It also leads on a 35B mixture of experts, to our knowledge the first trellis-coded result on such a model. With no rotation to undo, our decoder is the fastest of the three at all tested batch sizes and bit widths.

摘要：最佳的兩位元權重量化器，像是 QTIP 和 Proteus，通過隨機正交變換旋轉每個權重矩陣，這必須在每個解碼步驟中被撤銷，然後在歐幾里得搜索下用樹狀碼或格碼進行編碼；層海森矩陣僅通過編碼區塊之間的錯誤反饋進入。我們顯示這會導致部分海森矩陣未被使用。錯誤反饋將損失轉換為每個坐標四捨五入誤差的加權總和，其權重為海森矩陣 LDL 分解的對角線，現有的量化器計算但從不讀取。我們將這些權重放入 Viterbi 分支度量中，因此搜索遵循每個編碼區塊內的曲率。這也解釋了旋轉：它消除了這種區塊內變異，因此在本地基底中的加權和旋轉是替代品。在三個模型上，加權的本地搜索與全維隨機哈達瑪德的準確度相差約一點，而旋轉後的加權增益不大。在這個搜索的基礎上，我們構建了 CurveTQ，一個沒有旋轉的樹狀編解碼器，它使用分解的尺度場和封閉形式的分位數表來處理權重的幅度和邊際形狀，並為每個編碼區塊存儲一個起始狀態，以便樹狀編碼器可以適應錯誤反饋帶入的殘差。在兩位元的情況下，CurveTQ 在三個 4-8B 指令模型上的平均下游準確度比 QTIP 和 Proteus 高出 1-3 點，即使在兩者都給予我們的起始狀態後，僅此一項就能使任一基線提升 1-3 點。它在 35B 專家混合模型上也領先，據我們所知，這是這樣模型上第一個樹狀編碼的結果。由於沒有需要撤銷的旋轉，我們的解碼器在所有測試的批量大小和位寬中都是三者中最快的。

##### **Do Vision Models Learn Physical Constraints or Rendering Shortcuts? A Counterfactual Benchmark for Grounded Physical Consistency**
2610.09205v1 by M. Moein Esfahani, Sepehr Salem, Mohammed Alser, Vince Calhoun

Modern image editing models can satisfy a text instruction while breaking the physics of the edited scene. A new object may cast no shadow, a mirror may fail to reflect visible geometry, or an object may float above a surface that should support it. We study physical plausibility diagnosis, detecting whether an edited image violates scene physics, naming the violation type, localizing the affected region, and explaining the failure in language. We introduce a counterfactual benchmark whose controlled synthetic component uses Mitsuba~3 to generate 5,500 images from 500 scene families. Each family contains one clean image and ten matched violations involving shadows, reflection, support, surface response, and occlusion. The renderer pipeline provides category labels, affected-region masks and boxes, scene metadata, and explanation targets. We use LLaVA-1.5-7B, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, and InternVL3.5-8B as diagnostic baselines rather than proposed methods. On a 1,650-image synthetic test set, the adapted baselines reach 64.0--67.8\% category macro-F1 on standard held-out scenes. For LLaVA-1.5-7B, category macro-F1 falls from 64.0\% on the standard split to 40.8\% under intervention shift. This gap shows that high in-distribution accuracy partly reflects cues tied to rendering and counterfactual construction.

摘要：現代影像編輯模型可以滿足文本指令，同時違反編輯場景的物理法則。新的物體可能不會投下陰影，鏡子可能無法反射可見的幾何形狀，或者一個物體可能懸浮在應該支撐它的表面上。我們研究物理合理性診斷，檢測編輯後的影像是否違反場景物理，命名違規類型，定位受影響區域，並用語言解釋失敗。我們引入了一個反事實基準，其受控的合成組件使用 Mitsuba~3 生成 5,500 張來自 500 個場景家族的影像。每個家族包含一張乾淨的影像和十個涉及陰影、反射、支撐、表面反應和遮擋的匹配違規。渲染管道提供類別標籤、受影響區域的掩碼和框、場景元數據和解釋目標。我們使用 LLaVA-1.5-7B、Qwen2.5-VL-7B 和 InternVL3.5-8B 作為診斷基準，而不是提出的方法。在一個包含 1,650 張影像的合成測試集上，調整後的基準在標準保留場景上達到 64.0--67.8\% 的類別宏觀 F1。對於 LLaVA-1.5-7B，類別宏觀 F1 從標準分割的 64.0\% 下降到介入轉移下的 40.8\%。這一差距顯示，高分佈內準確率部分反映了與渲染和反事實構建相關的線索。

#### Abstracts
##### **Quantifying privacy risks in foundation model representations of brain MRI**
2610.20494v1 by Imen Bajar, Héber Hwang Arcolezi, Antoine Boutet, Carole Frindel, Mohamed Maouche

Pretrained representations are increasingly used to share and reuse information from medical images without exposing the original scans, but it is unclear how much patient-specific information these representations retain. We investigate this question for brain magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) by evaluating five frozen pretrained encoders spanning different architectures, pretraining objectives, and training domains on two longitudinal T1-weighted datasets, OASIS-2 and MIRIAD. Under an attacker model with access to released embeddings, the corresponding public encoder, and auxiliary MRI scans, we evaluate patient linkage and sensitive-attribute inference. Across the evaluated representations, we observe substantial subject-specific information: rank-1 patient linkage reaches up to 99\% on MIRIAD and exceeds 90\% on OASIS-2 for the highest-risk representation, despite the absence of an identification objective during encoder training. We further show that the released embeddings retain information about a demographic attribute, with recorded-sex prediction reaching balanced accuracy of 90.9\% on OASIS-2 and 95.5\% on MIRIAD. Overall, our results demonstrate that replacing raw brain MRI with learned embeddings does not, by itself, eliminate patient-linkage or attribute-inference risk, and is therefore insufficient on its own to establish anonymity.

摘要：

##### **HEM-Med: A Hierarchical Agentic Memory Framework for Safe Multi-Center Medication Recommendation**
2610.20191v1 by Min Xiao, Jin Li, Shoujin Wang, Yeguang Zhen, Yishuo Li, Wenpeng Lu

Medication recommendation (MR) is critical for clinical decision support. While existing MR methods are mostly designed for single-center learning, where only one independent hospital is involved, multi-center MR with hospitals of different scales has become increasingly demanding in practice. In multi center settings, abundant data from large medical centers can improve recommendation performance for low-resource centers. Despite a few preliminary attempts on this task, two critical gaps remain. Gap 1: Existing multi-center methods fail to disentangle transferable cross-hospital clinical experience from hospital-specific prescribing patterns, limiting clinical feasibility and physician acceptance. Gap 2: They commonly overlook multi label safety constraints of MR, including drug-drug interaction risks and local formulary feasibility, which are essential for safe real-world deployment. To address these gaps, we propose HEM-Med, a novel hierarchical agentic memory framework for multi-center MR. To address Gap 1, we design a disentangled hierarchical memory module that builds patient-level medication context attribution records and disentangles them into cross hospital experience memory and hospital-specific residual memory. To address Gap 2, we introduce a safety-aware medication set generation module, which integrates dual-level experience retrieval with safety-aware set decoding to produce clinically relevant, safer, and locally adaptive medication recommendations. Experiments on a real-world multi-center eICU dataset show that HEM-Med outperforms representative transfer learning, LLM-based, and MR baselines in terms of both recommendation accuracy and safety.

摘要：

##### **Multimodal Medical Image Parsing with Task-Specific Low-Rank Adapters**
2610.19901v1 by Taha Sabir, Alberto Montero Molina, Thinh Quang Ly, Veronika Dmitrenko, Mohammad Yaqub, Tausifa Jan Saleem

Adapting a single vision-language model to medical image parsing requires training choices that reflect the demands of each task. We study task-specific adaptation with Qwen3.5-9B for FLARE 2026 Task 3, covering six task families and seven imaging modalities. Our system couples a shared, frozen 4-bit backbone with LoRA adapters selected from the input question. We customize adapter ranks and prompts, rebalance rare classification targets, and supplement regression training with dermatology classification examples. On the fixed internal holdout, the task-specific adapters achieve a dataset-macro balanced accuracy of 0.7791 for classification, a dataset-macro micro-F1 of 0.6286 for multi-label classification, a counting mean absolute error (MAE) of 11.73, a dataset-macro detection F1 of 0.8914, a dataset-macro regression MAE of 9.78, and a report-generation CRIMSON score of 0.8204. On the organizer's 1,703-example hidden-validation set, the submitted system achieves 0.8496 classification balanced accuracy, 0.5338 sample-averaged multi-label F1, and 14.5950 regression MAE. The final 30.1 GB container averages 3.1 seconds per case, including model loading, and produces identical outputs across repeated verification runs. The benefits vary across tasks, supporting the use of task-specific adapter configurations and image budgets within a shared medical vision-language model. Code is available at https://github.com/TahaSabir0/FLARE26-Hexapus.

摘要：

##### **DeepPV-MRF: End-to-End Partial-Volume Fraction Estimation from MR Fingerprinting Time Series**
2610.19894v1 by Tianyi Ding, Yang Gao, Zhuang Xiong, Feng Liu, Martijn A. Cloos, Jin Jin, Hongfu Sun

Background: Estimating subvoxel tissue fractions from magnetic resonance fingerprinting (MRF) is challenging. Conventional dictionary matching and segmentation pipelines can propagate errors from intermediate parametric maps into tissue fraction estimates.
  Purpose: We introduce DeepPV-MRF, a deep learning framework that directly estimates voxel-wise tissue fractions from raw MRF signals.
  Methods: A cascaded spatial-temporal network was trained on subject-derived Bloch simulations with radial k-space undersampling. The network combines temporal encoding and spatial U-Net refinement to estimate cerebrospinal fluid (CSF), gray matter (GM), and white matter (WM) fractions without intermediate T1/T2 maps. We compared DeepPV-MRF with partial-volume dictionary matching (PV-DM) and pseudoinverse estimation (PV-Pinv) using simulated and in vivo data.
  Results: DeepPV-MRF outperformed both methods across acquisition settings. In single-shot simulations, CSF/WM/GM mean absolute errors were 0.0418/0.0744/0.0757, versus 0.1281/0.2443/0.2958 for PV-DM and 0.2021/0.2793/0.2630 for PV-Pinv. Corresponding structural similarity values were 0.960/0.916/0.922. In vivo results showed sharper anatomical boundaries and smoother tissue transitions, while cross-shot analysis indicated improved consistency across volunteers.
  Conclusions: DeepPV-MRF enables accurate and efficient direct tissue fraction estimation from MRF signals, particularly with limited shots. Further validation is needed before clinical deployment.

摘要：

##### **PRISMATIC : Prescription Risk Inspection System for Multi-Agent Treatment Interaction in Clinical Decision-Making**
2610.19762v1 by Ziyang Ling, Shuyu Lu, Ziyi Chen

Medical prescribing errors for multimorbid patients, who require complex drug regimens, are a major patient safety concern. These errors often stem from fragmented decision-making across medical specialties, a critical coordination failure that leads to adverse drug events and rehospitalizations. To address this coordination challenge, we introduce PRISMATIC, a multi-agent architecture where specialized agents, each representing a workflow role, collaboratively generate and refine treatment plans. Operating on a blackboard architecture, the system first structures patient information from clinical notes, then iteratively develops safe, rational, and interpretable drug regimens. The agents' collaboration is governed by two novel adaptive coordination mechanisms: (1) Dynamic Self-Updating Weight Adjustment (DSWA), a long-term mechanism that adjusts the influence weights of agents based on historical case outcomes, enabling the system to adapt and trust more reliable specialists over time. (2) Discrepancy Feedback Precise Calibration (DFPC), a short-term mechanism where agents provide iterative feedback to resolve disagreements and eliminate prescription conflicts for the current patient. Experiments on patient records from the MIMIC-IV database show that PRISMATIC significantly reduces drug conflicts, improves prescription coverage, and enhances treatment consistency compared to strong LLM baselines. Our work demonstrates that structuring clinical co-treatment as a multi-agent coordination problem offers a promising path toward safer and more coherent multidisease management.

摘要：

##### **Learning When to Trust Medical Knowledge: Regression-Safe Routing for Clinical Reasoning**
2610.19663v1 by Hui Zhang, Yujia Zeng, Yuhao Xin

Clinical reasoning LLMs need external medical knowledge, but retrieved evidence and reasoning chains can be outdated, poisoned, or hallucinated. Harmful knowledge can cause regression: changing a correct answer into an incorrect one. We construct a medical knowledge-poisoning benchmark covering retrieved evidence and reasoning chains, with seven attack families filtered by a three-judge "subtle-but-wrong" vote and paired clean exams that separate poisoning effects from question difficulty. No fixed knowledge-use strategy is robust across these settings: strong RAG methods trade robustness to poisoned evidence against accuracy when evidence is useful. We formulate knowledge use as per-instance routing and introduce Med-TrustRouter, a lightweight controller that keeps the solver frozen. From leakage-free runtime signals, it estimates each action's expected correctness gain and uncertainty, preserving the base answer unless another action is confidently better. Across seven medical reasoning settings, Med-TrustRouter achieves 78.9 macro and 69.2 worst-case accuracy, matching the strongest competitor on macro accuracy while substantially improving the worst-case floor. Its gains transfer across solvers and evidence judges, unlike a hand-coded router using the same signals. Similar performance across linear, tree, and neural action-value heads suggests that the benefit comes from routing rather than estimator complexity. These results support learning when to trust external knowledge for robust clinical reasoning.

摘要：

##### **IMU-Bench: Benchmarking Wrist IMU Representations Across Kinematic, Semantic and Clinical Tasks**
2610.19481v1 by Yiquan Li, Aidan Bradshaw, Chenfeng Gao, Karan Ahuja

Wrist-worn inertial measurement units (IMUs) provide a common sensing modality for tasks ranging from activity and gesture recognition to pose estimation, inertial odometry and clinical motor assessment. Existing wearable benchmarks evaluate performance across multiple tasks, but leave open how supervision for one task affects transfer to others and whether these tasks benefit from a shared representation. We present IMU-Bench, a benchmark of ten tasks from eight datasets for studying IMU multi-task performance and cross-task transfer. We compare six self-supervised methods pretrained on unlabeled recorded and simulated wrist motion, evaluating classification, pose and velocity estimation, and IMU-language retrieval. We find that our masked autoencoder, InrtlWrist, achieves the best or joint-best frozen-probe performance on nine of ten tasks, and fine-tuning improves on training from scratch on nine of ten tasks, including full-body pose, odometry and retrieval. Across transfer and joint-training experiments, supervision that benefits one target can reduce performance on others, with supervised sources transferring within kinematic estimation while, under frozen probing, none approaches the pretrained encoder on language retrieval, and joint-training gains that come at a cost to the paired task. These findings distinguish broad downstream usefulness from complementary task supervision. Code, task cards and a leaderboard are publicly available.

摘要：

##### **Beyond ICD Codes: Semantic IDs for Generative Medical Coding**
2610.19301v1 by Yihang Cheng, Qian Zhang,  Yonghui,  Huang, Baoshuo Kan, Junyi Shen

Generative approaches to automatic International Classification of Diseases (ICD) coding use large language models (LLMs) to predict ICD codes from clinical documents. However, original ICD code strings are not ideal generation targets because the ICD hierarchy does not always reflect clinical relationships, and LLMs struggle to interpret and generate these codes. We introduce SemICD, a framework that trains LLMs to generate Semantic IDs (SIDs) instead. SemICD constructs SIDs by hierarchically clustering embeddings of textual code profiles, allowing semantically related codes to share prefixes while each complete SID uniquely identifies one ICD code. To align the SID tokens with clinical semantics, we employ two auxiliary tasks for bidirectional generation between textual code profiles and SIDs. Experiments on MIMIC-III and MIMIC-IV with four LLMs show that SemICD outperforms direct code generation, improving Micro-F1 by 1.34-8.00 points. Our code is available at https://github.com/youwillbethebest/SemICD.

摘要：

##### **Diagnosing Low-Confidence Patient Profiles via Localized Harmful Covariate Shift Detection**
2610.19178v1 by Olivier Lefebvre, Hakima Laribi, Dan Poenaru, Martin Vallières

Before deploying clinical prediction models, their reliability must be assessed to ensure safe use. Errors may arise from distribution shifts between training and deployment or from heterogeneous performance where the model systematically underperforms for certain patient subgroups. Yet most post-hoc diagnostic methods evaluate reliability only at the population level, checking whether the deployment data resemble the training data and whether the model performs well on average, without accounting for heterogeneity that may cause some patients to be disproportionately affected. We propose a unified diagnostic pipeline that combines both perspectives. MED3pa first discovers interpretable low-confidence patient profiles, and Detectron is then applied within each profile to test whether its degradation is associated with a detectable harmful covariate shift. Across three clinical prediction tasks and three injected subgroup shifts in MIMIC-IV, we find that shift detectability itself is informative. An injected age shift, undetectable at the population level, was nonetheless localized to specific degraded profiles, whereas sex- and race-related shifts were detected broadly and therefore offered little guidance for identifying the most affected patients.

摘要：

##### **Verification Turns Capability Gaps into Routing Decisions: Small Language Models for Clinical Documentation**
2610.19169v1 by Nina Fatehi, Meysam Ghaffari, Animesh Agarwal, Anushka Swarup, Carlos Morato

Clinical note generation from doctor--patient dialogue is high-volume and hallucination-intolerant: a fabricated laboratory value can create patient-safety risk, not a quality regression. We ask whether an agentic \emph{verification} architecture, rather than generator scale, can make a small language model viable here. Holding a four-evaluator verification stack and refinement policy fixed and swapping only the generator between a 4B-parameter model with a QLoRA adapter and a frontier baseline, the small model reaches $0.984$ claim-level factual entailment against $0.992$ and comes within $3.3$ points on completeness, generating entirely on-device. Its deficit is confined to \emph{inter-section structural fidelity}---arrangement rather than clinical comprehension, and the axis on which it also falls furthest below the corpus's reference notes. Because that deficit is an explicit verifier decision rather than a silent quality difference, it becomes a routing policy: run the small model by default, escalate what the verifier rejects. Measured end to end, that it achieved a similiar verified-rate acceptance to the frontier-only acceptance in this test set ($99.6\%$ vs.\ $98.4\%$ over $250$ encounters) while routing $62$, documenting three quarters without a frontier \emph{generation} call. Ablating the loop separates architecture from scale: unrefined, the small model is accepted on $26.8\%$ of encounters and the frontier model on $74.8\%$, so verification does the most work where the generator is weakest. We also contribute section-scoped refinement, which preserves unflagged sections by construction rather than instruction, and find the baseline is not uniformly stronger---its provider refused two trauma-related transcripts the on-device model handled and certified, one facet of a provider dependency a served adapter does not carry.

摘要：

##### **SMI: Ontology-Grounded Semantic IDs for Hierarchy-Aware Medical Concept Representation**
2610.18672v1 by Ziyang Song, Lia Shen, Yixuan Li, Qincheng Lu, Weihao Li, Haoran Xu, He Zhu, Xinyu Wang, Yue Li

Biomedical language models (LMs) provide strong representations for clinical tasks, but they often fail to capture the hierarchical structure among medical concepts. This limitation is especially pronounced for rare and fine-grained clinical codes in long-tailed vocabularies, where hierarchy-agnostic LM representations become less informative as concept frequency decreases. We introduce Semantic Medical Identifier (SMI), a training-free framework for constructing ontology-grounded Semantic IDs and hierarchy-aware medical concept representations. For each diagnosis, procedure, or medication concept, SMI defines its Semantic ID as the coarse-to-fine path through an expert-defined medical ontology. SMI then aggregates frozen biomedical LM embeddings across the corresponding ontology levels to form hierarchy-aware medical concept representations that preserve coarse-to-fine clinical semantics. The base SMI uses uniform aggregation across ontology levels, while a level-weighted variant selects global level weights on validation data. Our theoretical analysis shows that SMI yields more informative medical concept representations than hierarchy-agnostic LM embeddings, particularly for rare and fine-grained clinical concepts. Experiments show that SMI captures meaningful hierarchical structure and improves clinical relation prediction. On MIMIC-IV, SMI improves mortality prediction, readmission prediction, drug recommendation, and rare drug prediction. On eICU, SMI yields more robust patient representations under cross-hospital distribution shift.

##### **Geometric Calibration of Supervised Fine-Tuning**
2610.18634v1 by Jianzhu Bao, Jia-Chen Gu, Qingyuan Liu, Haozhen Zhang, Kai-Wei Chang, Nanyun Peng, Wenya Wang

Recent studies investigate the weight geometry through its spectral structure, which characterizes how the weights transform different input directions. Within this view, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is shown to induce dense updates that perturb the spectral geometry of pre-SFT weights along their principal directions, potentially disrupting the model's prior knowledge. These observations motivate using the geometry of pre-SFT weights as a reference for refining learned SFT updates. We study this perspective as Geometric Calibration and develop SPACE Subspace Projection for Adapted Checkpoint Enhancement), a lightweight post-hoc method that selectively reverts SFT update components within the principal subspace of the pre-SFT weights while preserving those in its orthogonal complement. SPACE is a data-free, training-free method that requires only the pre- and post-SFT checkpoints. We evaluate SPACE on Qwen2.5, Qwen3, Qwen3.5, Llama-3.1, and Olmo-3 models ranging from 2B to 14B, across mathematical, coding, and medical SFT, as well as several SFT variants. Overall, SPACE mitigates out-of-domain (OOD) capability regression while further improving in-domain (ID) performance over the uncalibrated SFT checkpoints. For math SFT, SPACE yields average ID/OOD gains of 2.85/2.26 points on Qwen2.5-7B, and 2.41/1.52 points on Qwen3-14B-Base, respectively. These results highlight how the geometric relationship between SFT updates and pre-SFT weights shapes the adapted model's capabilities and, more broadly, motivate geometry-aware parameter updates for continual model improvement.

##### **Task-evoked eye movements as candidate digital biomarkers of depression severity in adolescents and young adults**
2610.18494v1 by Youxiang Sun, Yujie Nie, Zhuoru Wu, Mengyuan Yu, Wenkong Wang, Xiang Li, Yumei Wang, Xin Ma, Lizhou Fan

Objective digital measures of depression severity could complement conventional symptom assessments and facilitate more timely clinical evaluation. Here, we investigated whether task-evoked eye movements provide interpretable behavioral markers of depression severity in adolescents and young adults. In this single-center, cross-sectional study, 155 participants aged 14--20 years completed emotional-spatial free-viewing and memory-guided recall tasks and were stratified by 17-item Hamilton Depression Rating Scale scores. We examined 19 prespecified eye-movement features across six task-informed domains using covariate-adjusted ordinal-trend models. Greater depression severity was associated with lower gaze-allocation entropy (standardized $β=-0.599$), fewer inter-area switches ($β=-0.526$), longer first target-entry latency ($β=0.572$), and lower target dwell proportion ($β=-0.678$; all false-discovery-rate-adjusted $q<0.001$). Emotion-specific attentional-bias measures were not associated with severity, whereas associations involving fixation dispersion weakened after adjustment for anxiety symptoms. In a complementary machine-learning analysis incorporating 44 candidate eye-movement features and demographic covariates, a three-stage cascade classifier achieved the highest four-class stratification performance (accuracy, 0.665; macro-F1, 0.670; area under the receiver operating characteristic curve, 0.869), outperforming single-stage and two-stage approaches. Together, these findings identify altered visual exploration and memory-guided gaze control as candidate digital biomarkers of depression severity, rather than generalized negative attentional bias. These results support further prospective evaluation of task-evoked eye tracking as a complementary approach for timely severity assessment and clinical decision support.

##### **LiFUSE: Frequency-Spatial Unified Self-Supervised Pretraining for Liver Disease Analysis on Non-contrast CT**
2610.18243v1 by Hao Zhang, Yi Huang, Wei Liu, Yuan Gao, Zeju Li, Xu Han, Yu Shi, Ling Zhang, Ke Yan

Given the growing global burden of liver tumors and diffuse liver diseases, non-contrast computed tomography (NCCT) is increasingly used for opportunistic screening because of its low cost and favorable safety profile. However, downstream tasks such as tumor detection and fine-grained disease diagnosis remain challenging due to NCCT's inherently low tissue contrast, lack of hemodynamic enhancement, and complex anatomical background. These limitations make subtle, heterogeneous, and multifocal hepatic lesions difficult to identify. Although large-scale self-supervised pretraining can learn robust representations from unlabeled NCCT scans, conventional voxel-wise reconstruction does not fully exploit the complementary information available in NCCT. Under weak NCCT contrast, low-level intensity patterns become less informative for pathology recognition, limiting the capture of lesion-specific discriminative features. In this paper, we propose the Liver Frequency-Spatial Unified Self-Supervised Pretraining (LiFUSE). The frequency branch applies random band-stop masking and spectrum reconstruction to encourage the model to capture subtle differences between lesions and surrounding tissues, while the spatial branch performs hybrid-granularity masked volumetric reconstruction to accommodate lesions of diverse sizes and morphologies. Finally, a cross-domain consistency scheme couples the spatial and frequency branches, yielding more discriminative representations for hepatic structures and lesions. Extensive experiments on three clinically relevant tasks across internal cohorts and a public target-domain transfer setting show that LiFUSE consistently outperforms strong baselines. These results suggest that unified frequency-spatial learning can improve liver disease analysis on NCCT, highlighting its potential for large-scale screening.

##### **Mitigating Cross-Modal Hallucinations by Correcting Modality Misrouting in Omnimodal LLMs**
2610.18141v1 by Yu Mi, Licheng Pan, Jiayi Zhu, Qirui Liu, Yitong Qiao, Changtong Xu, Tao Wei, Fei Sun, Zhixuan Chu, Kui Ren

Omnimodal large language models (OLLMs) are susceptible to cross-modal hallucinations under audio-visual interference. We find that, for a substantial fraction of incorrect joint predictions, the question-relevant unimodal branch can still recover the correct answer, suggesting that task-relevant evidence can remain recoverable from the relevant modality even when it is not reflected in the joint prediction. We refer to this recoverable failure pattern as \emph{modality misrouting}. We propose CaRe (Conflict-aware Residual Intervention), a training-free inference-time method for identifying and correcting modality misrouting. CaRe uses a text-only modality probe to identify a candidate modality-specific counterfactual branch, and flags potential misrouting when its prediction conflicts with the original joint prediction. For such cases, CaRe computes a residual direction from the discrepancy between the counterfactual and fused representations and uses it to steer the original multimodal state toward the relevant modality evidence, while retaining both modalities in the original audio-visual inference path. The intervention strength is adapted based on the confidence of the counterfactual branch. Experiments on CMM and AVHBench across two OLLMs show that CaRe consistently outperforms the evaluated inference-time hallucination-mitigation baselines.

##### **Chain of Thought Monitoring Can Fail Even When CoT Is Necessary**
2610.18105v1 by Lennart Bürger, Piotr Komorowski, Moritz Weckbecker, Evgenii Kortukov, Aakriti Jain, Sebastian Lapuschkin, Wojciech Samek

Chain of Thought (CoT) monitoring is used by frontier labs to detect misbehaviour of AI systems. Yet, its reliability remains poorly understood. We attribute this to the lack of a successful organizing principle that leads to generalizable conclusions about monitoring reliability in different regimes. Recent work attributes monitoring failures to the CoT not being necessary for the answer, and argues that CoT becomes monitorable when it is necessary. We show that this binary necessary/not-necessary organizing principle is too coarse by demonstrating that monitoring failures persist even if the CoT is necessary for the answer. In response, we introduce a more fine-grained organizing principle based on a hierarchy of four CoT necessity levels. GUIDE offers a practical framework for timely, trustworthy, expert-centric guideline development and validates computationally augmented consensus.

Repairing every collision (Rescue mode) makes the trace exactly auditable but ranks no path higher; tolerating collisions (Don't Care mode) yields a path-integrated confidence, CR$_2$, that silently favours direct routes. This audit-valuation complementarity is a formal counterpart of Polanyi's claim that tacit and explicit knowledge are not sharply divided. ##### **MedHarnessBench: A Unified Benchmark for Healthcare Agent Evaluation**

Healthcare agents must transform longitudinal clinical evidence into reliable actions through heterogeneous tools and stateful workflows, conditions under which model-centric systems may lose context, misuse tools, fail to verify consequential transitions, or cross authorization boundaries. These results demonstrate that healthcare-specific runtime support can substantially improve both task completion and execution quality, while revealing failures that terminal outcomes alone cannot capture.   Performance was poor across all systems, and the failures were shared: different vendors missed the same findings and converged on the same wrong plans. From the experts' notes we catalogue 18 recurring failure modes in five families. The models stop reading an image at the first finding. They give generic plans and lean toward over-treatment. They trust a misleading history over correct imaging. Their answers vary across systems and runs, and change with question format. Failing answers were delivered fluently and without any expressed uncertainty. Four cases are released in full; the remaining 28 are held out, and we invite frontier AI labs and healthcare AI companies to evaluate their models against them (research@futrbridge.com)

EEG is not an arbitrary multichannel signal: each channel records the voltage difference between two scalp electrodes. So, real recordings satisfy exact linear identities among their channels. Current generators invariably violate these identities, yet this has gone unnoticed. We therefore propose MontageFlow, a flow-matching model that enforces these identities by restricting its output to signals that satisfy them. We find that ensuring validity improves fidelity too. Furthermore, to retain the EEG characteristics that oversmoothing washes out, we also condition the model on a physiological state vector encoded from the whole EEG window. For generation, this vector is drawn from a prior fitted in latent space. ##### **Visualizing the World Model in an Agent's Value Function**
2610.14186v1 by Shawheen Naderi, Fatemeh Akherati, Yubei Chen

Could model-free reinforcement learning algorithms actually be learning a model of the environment? In this work, we apply counterfactual interventions which hold everything about a state fixed, besides a targeted change, to explore if the value function of a Proximal Policy Optimization agent forms a world model of the Crafter environment. Despite only being trained to optimize reward, we discover that the value function learns concepts such as the rules governing when a diamond is collectible, water and food having an impact on health, heuristics regarding inventory, and more. Finally, we discuss the safety implications of our visualizations and some shortcomings of current reinforcement learning agents that will prevent us from building truly advanced machine intelligence.

When asked what would have happened if a doctor had not recommended surgery, the model output should retain the patient's medical history and modify only the consequences of the decision. This requires causal understanding: a model must determine, without being explicitly instructed to, what events are affected by the hypothetical change and what should remain fixed. This skill is central to human reasoning, especially in safety-critical domains like healthcare or law. By contrast, the task of associational text generation merely generates text statistically associated with the altered condition.
