We introduce the Counterfactual Benchmark (CoBe), a testbed for models' zero-shot ability to counterfactually edit a given textual scenario when prompted to do so in a conversationally natural way. CoBe contains 2000 questions spanning healthcare, science and engineering, business, and everyday scenarios. We test a suite of frontier and smaller models, with frontier systems achieving an average score of $58.05\%$. Our findings indicate that models frequently default to associational generation rather than preserving the causal structure of the original scenario. Moreover, performance does not scale consistently with either model size or output token length, suggesting that the failure may reflect model training objectives rather than insufficient model capacity.

摘要：反事實文本編輯是根據假設性變更修訂給定文本的能力，同時保留不受該變更因果影響的事實。當被問及如果一位醫生沒有建議手術會發生什麼時，模型的輸出應保留病人的病歷，僅修改決策的後果。這需要因果理解：模型必須在未被明確指示的情況下判斷哪些事件受到假設性變更的影響，哪些應保持不變。這項技能對於人類推理至關重要，特別是在醫療或法律等安全關鍵領域。相比之下，關聯文本生成的任務僅僅是生成與變更條件統計相關的文本。在醫療範例中，關聯性回應可能會使病人的症狀變得不那麼嚴重，因為輕微病例與不進行手術相關聯。這樣的回應產生了一個合理的情境，但不是給定情境的反事實版本。先前的反事實文本數據集並未明確區分這些推理模式。我們引入了反事實基準（CoBe），這是一個測試模型在被提示以自然對話方式進行反事實編輯時的零-shot能力的測試平台。CoBe包含2000個問題，涵蓋醫療、科學與工程、商業和日常情境。我們測試了一系列前沿和較小的模型，前沿系統的平均得分為$58.05\%$。我們的發現表明，模型經常默認為關聯生成，而不是保留原始情境的因果結構。此外，性能並未隨著模型大小或輸出標記長度的一致性擴展，這表明失敗可能反映模型訓練目標而非模型容量不足。

Conformal prediction offers a distribution-free coverage guarantee, making it especially attractive for clinical applications. Standard conformal prediction, however, provides such guarantees only at the population level, and its prediction sets can exhibit coverage disparities across clinically important subgroups. A natural remedy is to calibrate within predefined groups. However, this can require access to sensitive subgroup attributes and is prone to a worst-group bottleneck: protecting the most difficult subgroup can inflate prediction sets for all, increasing cognitive burden on decision makers. To this end, we propose Stochastic Grouping Conformal Prediction (SGCP), a conformal framework for subgroup-reliable uncertainty quantification. It learns a stochastic grouping map that allows each sample to draw calibration information from others with similar calibration behavior, yielding a local score law that boosts reliability across subpopulations. We prove that SGCP retains the standard coverage guarantee. Experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks show that it consistently reduces subgroup coverage gaps while achieving smaller or comparable prediction set sizes relative to existing baselines.

摘要：解決高維結構模型更新問題需要一種能夠在具有相關參數的複雜非凸地形中導航的算法。現有的混合進化算法通常依賴於靜態架構或固定的切換規則，導致搜索階段不連貫。為了解決這個問題，本研究提出了一種由深度強化學習主導的動態DE-CMAES協調算法（DRL-DCO），其中基於深度確定性策略梯度（DDPG）的演員-評論者代理持續主導進化過程，作為一個單一的統一系統，而不是算法的機械串聯。代理在進度感知的狀態表示和多樣性知情的獎勵指導下，靈活地在基於差異向量的差分進化（DE）探索和協方差引導的CMA-ES利用之間重新分配計算資源，同時共同調節種群大小、精英保留和重啟機制，以逃避局部最優。這使得DRL-DCO能夠在世代之間自主轉換探索主導、利用主導和混合策略的模式。在訓練階段之後，訓練過的演員可以在無需監督的推斷模式下運行，其中內部化的策略自主協調DE和CMA-ES控制，僅通過前向推斷從觀察到的搜索狀態進行，而無需評論評估或權重更新，從而實現更快的部署，同時保持完全的有效性。在高維單目標優化基準和IASC-ASCE結構健康監測基準上進行驗證，DRL-DCO在收斂準確性和穩健性方面超越了最先進的自適應和混合進化算法，以及單一運營商的DRL主導基準。

摘要：憂鬱症沒有診斷性的血液測試。語言模型承諾提供不知疲倦且一致的評估，但準確的評估者能否對個體達成共識？我們預先註冊了880名語言模型評估者，交叉使用11個開放模型與提示和評分選擇，並將其應用於189次訪談，對照八項患者健康問卷。模型選擇解釋了30.0%的總症狀分數變異，穩定的參與者差異為10.5%。兩名隨機抽取的評估者，其接收者操作特徵曲線下面積（AUC）>= 0.70，對40%的參與者的篩查決策存在分歧，平均而言。平均過度評分決定了有多少被標記，然而能力相等的評估者對約五分之一的參與者做出了不同的選擇。對86個新訪談的鎖定分析重現了主要的預先註冊發現。對40名標記參與者的探索性重新校準將準確度從約60%提高到75%，並將分歧減半，仍然有五分之一的參與者做出了不同的決定。校準修復了大部分的評估者依賴性，但未能確保對個體的共識。

##### **Knee3DVLM: Dual-Sequence Full-Volume Vision-Language Modeling for Comprehensive Knee MRI Assessment**
2610.08482v2 by Maryam Baizhigitova, Andrew Seohwan Yu, Po-Hao Chen, Naveen Subhas, Sixu Chen, Xinxin Wang, Kunio Nakamura, Richard Lartey, Xiaojuan Li, Mingrui Yang

Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly being applied to three-dimensional medical imaging, but their application to knee MRI remains limited, particularly for interpreting the complementary sequences used in clinical practice. We introduce Knee3DVLM, a sequence-aware VLM that uses full-volume DESS and fluid-sensitive TSE MRI to predict 57 anatomically resolved binary diagnostic targets derived from the MRI Osteoarthritis Knee Score (MOAKS) for structured reporting. We evaluated DESS-only, TSE-only, and paired DESS-TSE configurations using subject-disjoint Osteoarthritis Initiative partitions. In a held-out cohort of 1,074 examinations, the fused model achieved 72.98% average accuracy, 71.17% balanced accuracy, 78.96% mean ROC-AUC, and 78.74% macro ROC-AUC, the highest values among the three configurations. In a secondary multiclass analysis aligned with the released 3DReasonKnee cohort, Knee3DVLM was numerically higher than the strongest reported 3DReasonKnee configuration across five pathology categories. These findings support dual-sequence full-volume modeling for comprehensive knee MRI assessment.

摘要：視覺-語言模型 (VLMs) 正在越來越多地應用於三維醫學影像，但其在膝關節 MRI 的應用仍然有限，特別是在解釋臨床實踐中使用的互補序列方面。我們介紹了 Knee3DVLM，一種序列感知的 VLM，使用全體積 DESS 和流體敏感的 TSE MRI 來預測 57 個從 MRI 骨關節炎膝關節評分 (MOAKS) 中衍生的解剖學解析二元診斷目標，以進行結構化報告。我們使用主題不重疊的骨關節炎倡議分區評估了僅 DESS、僅 TSE 和配對 DESS-TSE 配置。在一個包含 1,074 次檢查的保留隊列中，融合模型達到了 72.98% 的平均準確率、71.17% 的平衡準確率、78.96% 的平均 ROC-AUC 和 78.74% 的宏 ROC-AUC，這是三種配置中最高的值。在一項與釋出的 3DReasonKnee 隊列對齊的次級多類別分析中，Knee3DVLM 在五個病理類別中數值上高於報告的最強 3DReasonKnee 配置。這些發現支持雙序列全體積建模以進行全面的膝關節 MRI 評估。

##### **Sensor Geometry as a Flow-Matching Prior for Multi-Channel Brain Signals**
2610.08355v1 by Jaedong Hwang

Flow-matching models start from an isotropic Gaussian source, the standard choice when the correlation structure of the data is unknown in advance. For multi-channel brain recordings, however, part of this structure is known in advance. Electrodes sit at fixed positions on the head, and volume conduction through the skull and scalp makes nearby electrodes co-vary in a way that is shared across subjects. Existing EEG generative models nonetheless leave the network to learn this from scratch. We put this structure into the source instead. From the sensor coordinates alone, we build a k-nearest-neighbor graph and take a graph-Matérn function of its Laplacian as the source covariance, so the flow starts from spatially coherent patterns rather than channel-independent noise. The change adds no learned parameters, works with any coupling and any drift network, and uses the same three hyperparameters on every dataset. Across eight EEG datasets and four flow-matching methods, the graph-Matérn source lowers the spectral discrepancy between generated and real signals in the five clinical bands (PSD-KL) on most datasets. PSD-KL falls by 12% to 17% in geometric mean over datasets depending on the method and by up to 40% on PhysioNet-MI, the densest montage. We show that the improvement stems from the spatial eigenvectors of the local graph of sensor positions, since randomizing the eigenvectors while preserving the eigenvalue spectrum eliminates the gain. Furthermore, a prior fitted directly to the empirical data covariance performs worse than isotropic noise. The same construction applies unchanged to MEG, intracranial EEG with patient-specific grids, and a traffic-sensor network, lowering PSD-KL for every method on each. https://jd730.github.io/projects/GraphPrior

摘要：流匹配模型從各向同性的高斯源開始，這是當數據的相關結構事先未知時的標準選擇。然而，對於多通道腦電圖記錄，這部分結構是事先已知的。電極固定在頭部的特定位置，通過顱骨和頭皮的體積傳導使得相鄰的電極以一種在不同受試者之間共享的方式共同變化。現有的腦電圖生成模型仍然讓網絡從零開始學習這一點。我們將這一結構直接放入源中。僅根據傳感器坐標，我們構建了一個k最近鄰圖，並將其拉普拉斯算子的圖-馬特恩函數作為源協方差，因此流從空間一致的模式開始，而不是通道獨立的噪聲。這一變化不增加任何學習參數，適用於任何耦合和任何漂移網絡，並在每個數據集上使用相同的三個超參數。在八個腦電圖數據集和四種流匹配方法中，圖-馬特恩源降低了生成信號與真實信號之間在五個臨床頻段（PSD-KL）的頻譜差異，對於大多數數據集而言。根據方法的不同，PSD-KL在數據集上的幾何平均下降了12%到17%，在PhysioNet-MI這個最密集的佈局上則下降了最多40%。我們顯示，這一改進源於傳感器位置的局部圖的空間特徵向量，因為在保留特徵值譜的同時隨機化特徵向量會消除增益。此外，直接擬合到經驗數據協方差的先驗表現不如各向同性噪聲。相同的構造不變地適用於MEG、具有患者特定網格的顱內腦電圖以及交通傳感器網絡，降低每種方法在每個數據集上的PSD-KL。 https://jd730.github.io/projects/GraphPrior

#### Abstracts
##### **Modeling and Understanding Neural Weights of Humanoid Policies with Diffusion**
2610.20606v1 by Huixuan Cheng, Bike Zhang, Shankar Sastry, Koushil Sreenath

Recent advances in robot learning have driven rapid progress in humanoid control, enabling increasingly agile behaviors in the real world. However, humanoid control networks are typically obtained through conventional training procedures, while comparatively little attention has been given to directly modeling the distribution of their learned weights and generating functional networks in weight space. In this work, we investigate whether high-performing humanoid control networks can be generatively modeled directly through their weights. We adopt a latent diffusion framework that couples a linear autoencoder with a Diffusion Transformer. The autoencoder compresses actor network parameters into a compact latent space, and the Diffusion Transformer models the distribution of these latents. Trained on networks collected from reinforcement learning for the Unitree G1 robot, our approach generates deployable networks that perform various motions in the real world, including walking, dancing, and spin kick. Beyond generation, we further study the learned weight space to better understand the structure captured by these humanoid control networks. We identify a structural principle in the first-layer weights that consistently reflects the robot's kinematic prior. Notably, this kinematic prior emerges under both Reinforcement Learning (RL) and Imitation Learning (IL) training, suggesting that it is not specific to a particular optimization process but may instead reflect common knowledge associated with humanoid embodiment. Together, these results demonstrate the feasibility of generative modeling in humanoid network weight space, while providing initial insights into the information encoded by their learned weights. Additional results are available on our project website: https://humanoid-weight-diffusion.github.io/

摘要：

##### **Co-Evolving Acting and Forecasting for Language Agents**
2610.20597v1 by Haofei Yu, Yixing Chen, Yining Zhao, Xuyan Ye, Yuxiang Liu, Zhigang Hua, Yan Xie, Shuang Yang, Jiaxuan You

Training language agents is bottlenecked by the high cost of long-horizon rollouts, yet standard agentic reinforcement learning (RL) learns only from a terminal scalar reward, leaving the supervision in intermediate steps largely unused. Prior work exploits this supervision through value targets that estimate per-step success probabilities or observation targets that fit environment response. Both are hard to construct well: (1) value targets come from the same single scalar signal for each step in the trajectory, and (2) observation targets, such as web pages and terminal logs, are long and noisy and reach only one step ahead. We propose \name, which adds a third kind of multi-step action targets. On successful on-policy trajectories, \name trains the policy backbone to generate the action sequences that follows each history. Each action is also supervised when forecast from the preceding histories, forming a temporal ensemble that the shared backbone absorbs during training, and this enables acting and forecasting to co-evolve. The policy still acts one step at a time at inference, with no overhead. Across five benchmarks (ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, WebShop, $τ^2$-bench, and AppWorld), \name (1) achieves stronger aggregate performance than GRPO and RL baselines with predictive auxiliary targets across simulated environments, while improving overall performance on both realistic applications; (2) delivers up to $1.9\times$ and $2.0\times$ higher sample efficiency than ECHO and GRPO, respectively, on ScienceWorld; and (3) achieves these gains with a single policy backbone, without additional models, separate pretraining, or inference overhead. The codebase is available at https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

摘要：

##### **Your Transformer Is Secretly Elastic**
2610.20588v1 by Walter Simoncini, Dheeraj Varghese, Gertjan J. Burghouts, Cees G. M. Snoek

Scaling up transformers has produced models with broad capabilities, deployed across heterogeneous hardware and fluctuating workloads. Yet, model families offer only a few fixed sizes (e.g., S, B, L), forcing users to commit to a model based on hardware and latency constraints. This rigidity is often suboptimal under varying query loads. We introduce a retraining-free method that elastifies pre-trained transformers into a single model that adapts on-the-fly across a continuum of computational budgets. Our key contribution is a differentiable relaxation of the functional unit-ranking problem: unlike prior work, which optimizes a single importance weight per MLP block or attention head, we rank every individual MLP neuron and attention head jointly, on a common scale, by minimizing the model's loss under soft pruning across many sparsity targets, all at once. Across 27 vision and text transformer encoders ranging from 22M to 8B parameters, our method yields smooth degradation up to 60% sparsity and outperforms one-shot baselines under deep pruning. For example, pruning DINOv3 ViT-H+/16 to 50% sparsity removes 427M parameters while reducing ImageNet-1k linear-probing accuracy by only 0.8%, without the need for weight correction or retraining. At 60% sparsity, a pruned AugReg ViT-B/16 model achieves up to $1.80\times$ higher throughput and $1.26\times$ lower single-query latency on an A100 GPU. Elastic models are produced in minutes, with construction time scaling sub-linearly with parameter count.

摘要：

##### **Price of Anarchy of First-Price Auctions in Agentic Markets**
2610.20587v1 by Shubhendu Trivedi, Jacob Eisenstein, Alekh Agarwal, Ian Gemp, Michael Kaisers, Adam Fisch

An agentic market is a decentralized serving-time market where AI agents bid to claim each user prompt. An agent's bid is based on its expected value for the prompt, which is the expectation of the reward for its response minus its inference costs. First-price auctions (FPAs), in which the highest bidder pays its bid and receives the prompt, are a natural way to allocate prompts in such a market, since they are simple and transparent to run, and do not require central knowledge of private capabilities and costs. However, FPAs are not truthful: participants may bid less than their true value in the hope of a larger profit, and the prompt may then be allocated to someone other than the bidder who values it the most. This welfare loss is quantified by the $\textit{price-of-anarchy}$ (PoA). In this paper, we argue that agentic markets are likely to possess features that reduce the price-of-anarchy. The first is a $\textit{competitive floor}$, which occurs when multiple bidders usually value the prompt above some baseline share of the optimal welfare (e.g., because the best models are often distilled into cheaper alternatives, which perform similarly). We show that competition among them then limits the welfare loss, which can be driven to zero as the floor rises. The second feature is $\textit{near-independence}$ of values after conditioning on the prompt, which occurs when most of the predictable variation in the expected value is determined by the difficulty of the prompt. As the value distribution approaches conditional independence, we can derive an efficiency guarantee that approaches a factor of $1 - 1/e^2$ of the optimal welfare. Thus, both features give rise to welfare lower bounds that are significantly better than the well-known general case of $1 - 1/e$. We validate these results empirically using auctions with strategic bidders simulated on the EmbedLLM prompt routing dataset.

摘要：

##### **Player-1: Learning to Play from Synthetic Experience**
2610.20584v1 by Mingyu Ouyang, Yiran Wang, Siyuan Hu, Xingyilang Yin, Huiqiang Sun, Guangzhi Wang, Junfu Pu, Cheng Cheng, Honghao Chen, Ying Shan, Mike Zheng Shou, Hwee Tou Ng

Computer-use agents have demonstrated strong capabilities in interpreting screens and executing keyboard and mouse actions. However, games, with their visually rich worlds and diverse mechanics, remain challenging and offer a demanding next step toward general digital control. Current game-agent learning pipelines often rely on large-scale demonstrations with limited coverage of dynamic game states, while scalable environments for interactive training remain difficult to obtain. We introduce Player-1, a post-training framework for learning game control beyond demonstrations through synthetic experience. We extend the computer-use action space with relative pointer motion, sustained inputs, and composite actions. For supervised initialization, we fine-tune computer-use models on 6,044 curated gameplay trajectories that pair recorded actions with task instructions and visual reasoning. We construct Player-1-Gym, sixteen synthetic 2D and 3D environments designed around control demands shared across games. Programmatically verifiable rewards and randomized observations and control dynamics support multi-environment reinforcement learning with PPO. The resulting Player-1-9B and Player-1-35B-A3B improve MineDojo-300 success over their SFT policies by 27.8 and 17.7 percentage points, respectively. Player-1-9B reaches 38.33% success compared with 12.63% for the strongest evaluated baseline, with further gains over SFT on ViZDoom-10 and GameWorld. Further analyses show that broader synthetic environment coverage improves transfer, while the use of composite actions that combine multiple keyboard and mouse inputs increases during RL. We demonstrate that learning from synthetic experience substantially improves game control beyond supervised initialization, with gains that generalize beyond the RL training suite.

摘要：

##### **Latent Compaction with Test-Time Training**
2610.20582v1 by Yingfa Chen, Xingyu Shen, Chaojun Xiao, Xu Han

Long contexts enlarge key--value (KV) caches, increasing memory use and attention costs during generation. Compressing these caches must preserve information for unseen questions. We introduce **Latent Compaction**, which learns a compact document-specific cache through test-time training. We initialize cache entries using reconstruction importance, teacher attention, and key-space diversity. Training first fits attention outputs and log-normalizers to those of the full cache, with reconstruction-selected entries held fixed. It then jointly optimizes the entire cache through end-to-end distillation from the full-cache model. Model weights remain frozen throughout, and training uses only generic prompts about the document, without access to downstream questions or answers. Experiments on RULER, LongBench, and Phonebook Lookup demonstrate substantial gains over budget-matched compression baselines. On a RULER, for example, our method achieves 96.7% accuracy at 32$\times$ compression, compared with 40.0% for KVZip+. The method also transfers across model scales and families, with retrieval gains on both Qwen3 and MiniCPM5. These results show that treating the KV cache as a neural network and optimizing it with test-time training can preserve retrieval accuracy under aggressive compression. The code is released at https://www.github.com/THUNLP/latent-compaction

摘要：

##### **One Task, Many Interfaces: Simple Cross-Interface Consistency Training Improves LLM Agent Robustness**
2610.20576v1 by Artin Tajdini, Manpreet Kaur, Peng Qi

Post-training binds the competence of an LLM agent to the interface it was trained on. Semantics-preserving perturbations of that interface, such as renaming an action or changing the notation, leave the underlying task unchanged yet make the agent worse at it. Scoring each sample independently means interface randomization only broadens this dependence from a single interface to the training interface distribution, so out-of-distribution perturbations still degrade the agent. We propose Cross-Interface Policy Regularization (CIPR), a self-supervised objective that replaces action names with uninformative identifiers and presents each training sample under two interfaces in which equivalent actions are identified by construction. CIPR penalizes the KL divergence between the action distributions induced under the two interfaces, so the policy behaves consistently across them. We evaluate CIPR in both supervised and reinforcement-learning post-training, where it improves robustness. On agentic function calling, held-out interface perturbations reduce the accuracy of a post-trained agent by nearly 25%, and CIPR recovers 10.5% while leaving accuracy on the unchanged interface intact.

摘要：

##### **Beyond LLM Judge: Mechanical Verification for Agentic Autoformalization**
2610.20573v1 by Anna Mei, Michael R. Douglas

We introduce a novel method for verifying Lean formalizations that goes beyond LLM-judged faithfulness. Unlike most verification methods, which rely on LLMs or trained models, our method combines LLM-based semantic review with mechanical numerical and vacuity checks, providing checkable feedback and detecting errors missed by LLM judges. We apply this method, along with our Extract-Verify-Proof autoformalization pipeline, to construct the Formalized Library of Mathematical Functions (FLMF), a partial formalization of the NIST Digital Library of Mathematical Functions (DLMF). On three labeled datasets, our method achieves the lowest accepted-error rate among the evaluated methods, measured as the fraction of incorrect statements accepted by the verification method. Expert review of the results also identifies translation and source defects in existing datasets. Code available at https://github.com/annamei1/FLMF-Ch7ErrFunc-Release.git.

摘要：

##### **Recovering Reliable Supervision via Critique-Guided On-Policy Distillation**
2610.20572v1 by Kaixiang Zhao, Tianrun Yu, Shangzhe Li, Yuxiao Yang, Amanda Hughes, Weitong Zhang, Taylor W. Killian

On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own rollouts with token-level supervision from a teacher, assuming that the teacher can reach a correct answer from any prefix the student writes. We call this property the recoverability of the teacher and show it fails on incorrect rollouts: the teacher follows student errors, although it recognizes them. We propose CriticOPD, using error recognition to restore recoverability. The teacher critiques an incorrect rollout, allowing the student to rewrite its solution following the error. The resulting output, including the critique as privileged information, is scored by the teacher while the student uses the revision without the critique for training. On two Qwen3 students and six mathematical reasoning benchmarks, CriticOPD improves OPD, with the largest gains on competition problems.

摘要：

##### **Manacá-1B-Instruct-2: Reproducible Safety Alignment for a Native Brazilian-Portuguese LLM. Negative DPO Results, a Controlled Composition Scan, and a Silent Failure Mode of the LLM Judge**
2610.20570v1 by Bruno Leonardo Santos Menezes, Carlos Leonardo Souza Cardoso, Fabio André Machado Porto

Brazilian Portuguese remains underserved by open-source language models, and the few that are safety-aligned rarely document what did not work or validate comparisons with statistical significance. We present Manacá-1B-Instruct-2, the second-generation post-training alignment of Manacá-1B (1.72B parameters, trained from scratch for PT-BR), with an SFT corpus of 291,223 examples. We document three DPO attempts, all negative, including a case where the training reward margin reaches 1.0 while free generation does not change at all, and show that safety came from a dedicated safety-SFT (LLM-jp AnswerCarefully style), refined over three iterations until closing the safety-helpfulness frontier. A controlled scan of the fraction of general data in the safety-SFT mix (10% to 60%) reveals a non-monotonic over-refusal curve with a knee point at 40-50%. The final model is compared against four external PT-BR models on the same items, with Wilson confidence intervals and paired McNemar tests with Holm-Bonferroni correction: the lower bound of its safety interval (94.1%) lies above the upper bound of all four baselines. We also report a methodological finding for LLM-judge evaluation: on two of six safety items in MT-Bench-PT, the judge categorically refuses to assign a score regardless of the evaluated response, silently reducing the effective N of that category in every prior report of the project. We complement this with an n-gram decontamination check and a Chinchilla-anchored compute-budget table that attributes the gap in general capability to pre-training scale rather than alignment methodology. All code, raw data, logs, and scripts that produce every reported number are publicly released.

摘要：

##### **Scaling Discovery Requires Forgetting**
2610.20563v1 by Shangyin Tan, Ben Zhou, Lakshya A Agrawal, Jialin Zhang, Yilin Chen, Qiuyang Mang, Donghyun Lee, Dennis Jacob, Koushik Sen, Alex Dimakis, Matei Zaharia

Automated research with LLM agents has shown promising results in various domains. Through iterative refinement and scored verifiers, LLM agents are capable of discovering new ideas. There are different ways to scale automated research: let one agent iterate for longer, run several agents in parallel, or further let the parallel agents communicate and build on one another's candidates. However, we find that all three strategies eventually plateau as the agents accumulate history. Each agent becomes anchored to its own past attempts, making fewer and smaller edits. Beyond this point, additional budget brings little further improvement. In this paper, we show that the key to scaling discovery further is to forget. Periodically discarding the search state, including every agent's session and the candidate history, and restarting from the best candidate found so far, unblocks the search and pushes the same budget further.
  We implement forgetting in a team of agents with communication and evaluate it on four tasks spanning heuristic programming, kernel optimization and model training. The results show up to 15% improvement compared to parallel agents, and up to 39% compared to a single agent. Our results suggest that, for open-ended discovery, what an agent is allowed to remember matters as much as how much compute it is given.

摘要：

##### **PhySGA: A Physics-Space Grounded Agentic Framework for Generalizable and Interactive 3D Composition**
2610.20560v1 by Weichen Lyu, Chong Xia, Mingyu Dong, Long Xu, Shengjun Zhang, Yueqi Duan

3D object composition is pivotal to digital content creation and robot simulation, while also serving as a critical testbed for physical intelligence in foundation models. Previous methods either leverage large language models to directly predict object coordinates, or utilize generative diffusion priors to synthesize composite images for visual alignment, both of which rely on static priors in digital spaces. However, these methods consistently struggle with complex compositions such as precise hanging, gravity-driven settling and hidden containment, as these tasks require interactive construction processes involving global planning, fine-grained alignment, and physical simulation. To address these challenges, we propose PhySGA, a Physics-Space Grounded Agentic Framework for 3D composition via agent exploration and physical simulation, enabling the universal interactive assembly of rigid, articulated, and deformable objects. Directly applying vanilla LLMs or VLMs to this task proves insufficient, as current models inherently lack grounded physical understanding and interactive construction experience. To overcome this limitation, we design a collaborative feedforward-feedback architecture: an Assembly Agent focuses on geometric and kinematic reasoning for global step planning and coarse adjustment, while a Critic Agent reasons over contact dynamics for simulator state diagnosis and fine-grained closed-loop correction. Furthermore, Action Routers map semantic planning and analysis onto executable physical primitives within the simulator, while a task-specific Self-Evolving Skill Library distills historical trial-and-error feedback into reusable experiences, boosting success rates and minimizing exploration attempts. Extensive experiments within Isaac Sim demonstrate that PhySGA significantly surpasses existing methods in relation fidelity, physical plausibility, and visual quality.

##### **PerSo: Investigating Facets of LLMs' Social Intelligence in Real-World Persuasion Scenarios**
2610.20548v1 by Ali Zamani, Soroush Esfahanian, Erfan Shahabi, Mohammad Taher Pilehvar, Azadeh Shakery, Yadollah Yaghoobzadeh

Understanding the social intelligence of large language models (LLMs) is increasingly important for applications involving human-AI interaction and multi-agent environments. Social intelligence comprises two aspects: cognitive intelligence, the ability to infer others' mental states, and behavioral intelligence, the ability to select appropriate social actions based on these inferences to achieve social goals. Persuasion provides a natural testbed in which both aspects are simultaneously at play, yet prior benchmarks typically evaluate them in isolation using synthetic scenarios. We introduce PerSo, a benchmark that evaluates both aspects on real-world persuasion dialogues from Reddit's ChangeMyView community, using an LLM-in-the-loop pipeline to construct the evaluation tasks, validated through human annotation. Our evaluation of diverse LLMs shows that performance generally scales with model size but varies across dimensions. The strongest LLM slightly surpasses the best observed human performance on the behavioral task, while the best human participant remains ahead on the cognitive task.

##### **Detection of Potato Plant Leaf Diseases using Convolutional Neural Networks**
2610.20544v1 by Haimanot Bitew Alemayehu, Simachew Melaku Ayalew, Chilotie Desalew Mengesha, Ayisheshim Almaw Bogale, Anteneh Birhan Yenew, Abrham Tegegne Bogale

Potato is an important food-security crop in Ethiopia, but its production is affected by foliar diseases, particularly early blight and late blight. This study develops a lightweight convolutional neural network (CNN) to classify potato leaves into three categories: healthy, early blight, and late blight. The proposed model was trained using 2,215 images from the PlantVillage dataset and evaluated on an independent field dataset containing 247 images collected from potato farms in Banja, Awi Zone, Ethiopia. On PlantVillage, the model achieved 99.04% training accuracy, 98.21% validation accuracy, and 98.66% test accuracy. On the field dataset, it achieved 90.28% accuracy. The study also compares the proposed CNN with VGG16 and MobileNetV2 in terms of classification performance and computational efficiency. The proposed model contains 446,787 trainable parameters, occupies approximately 1.70 MB, and requires an average of 5.58 seconds per training epoch in the reported experimental setup, compared with 9.96 seconds for VGG16 and 7.68 seconds for MobileNetV2. The results demonstrate the potential of a compact CNN for potato leaf disease classification and highlight the importance of independent field evaluation, where image conditions are more variable than in controlled benchmark datasets. The proposed approach may support preliminary disease identification for smallholder farmers in Ethiopia and similar agricultural settings.

##### **PrismaCoder: Pushing the Boundaries of Open Multimodal Code Intelligence through Decomposed Rubric Synthesis**
2610.20538v1 by Qiushi Sun, Jingyang Gong, Yang Liu, Qiaosheng Chen, Zhangyue Yin, Zixian Huang, Hanxu Hu, Jing Xiong, Fangzhi Xu, Haochen Tan, Haoli Bai, Lifeng Shang, Xiaoguang Li, Ben Kao, Qipeng Guo, Lingpeng Kong, Jiahui Gao

Multimodal code intelligence connects the symbolic world of programs with the visual world their execution creates. Interest in this capability is burgeoning, as models are increasingly asked to produce charts, webpages, animations, and semi-structured documents. Recently, proprietary models have pushed far ahead, producing interactive artifacts even from a text description or a screenshot alone. Yet their data and training remain closed, and open progress is held back by the quantity and quality of available data and by the pipelines and methods for producing it. Quality is the harder of the two, since a sample must be correct as a program, well built, and above all visually right once rendered. No existing signal covers all three. Execution often returns just a pass or fail, code similarity does not map to the visual output, and task-specific verifiers do not generalize. To push open progress further, we start from the data side with PrismaForge, a hierarchical data pipeline centered on a synthesized rubric that decomposes these factors into checkable criteria. The same rubric serves as both filter and reward: it curates the supervised corpus, then drives reinforcement learning on visual-coding tasks. Leveraging PrismaForge for data synthesis and filtering, we build PrismaCoder-2M, the most diverse open dataset for multimodal code intelligence and the largest to date. On the model side, we train PrismaCoder-9B and PrismaCoder-35B on this data and release both. They lead the open state of the art by a wide margin and rival leading proprietary systems. Our analyses further show that the proposed rubric-based pipeline improves multimodal coding performance across model backbones. We release our code and data at https://qiushisun.github.io/PrismaCoder-Home/.

##### **ActiveEvidence: Decision-Focused Evidence Graphs with Verifiable Acquisition Gaps**
2610.20532v1 by Miaobo Hu, Shuhao Hu, Xiaobo Guo, Xin Wang, Bokun Wang, Peng Zhang, Daren Zha, Jun Xiao

A multimodal agent must decide whether visual evidence is worth acquiring, which candidate or resolution to query, and when further interaction can no longer change its decision. We study this problem under a strict free-metadata/paid-observation boundary, stochastic vector costs, and a frozen execution trace, and treat it as decision-certifiable active evidence acquisition. A Counterfactual Evidence Interaction Graph (CEIG) represents unary evidence value, signed second-order interaction, and directed conditional harm while maintaining explicit score enclosures for unresolved interactions; a decision-weighted enclosure objective tightens the bounds that can change action identity. Gap-directed refinement evaluates only interactions that can displace the incumbent acquisition and stops once an $ε$-decision certificate shows that unresolved terms cannot change the selected action beyond a declared tolerance. The certificate is model-relative: it verifies policy-consistent optimality under the pinned learned score, and semantic quality is measured separately through post-freeze labels. Paired continuation rollouts learn the downstream value of paid previews, an online operational cost filter is separated from independent finite-policy-family trajectory certification, and an acquisition monitor with an independently implemented verifier binds every certificate to the frozen trace. On 640 cases split into 384/128/128 training, validation, and test cases, the controller reaches task score 0.842, grounding F1 0.816, evidence recall 0.873, 1.74 accepted calls, 286.5K visual tokens, and 742ms p95 latency. Dense replay of issued certificates shows 0.992 action agreement and a 0.988 certificate rate while evaluating 19.6 instead of 191.4 expensive interaction terms per decision.

##### **The Wealth of Agents: Scaling Agentic AI Across Developers**
2610.20518v1 by Austin W. Hanjie, Carlos E. Jimenez, Zirui Wang, Karthik Narasimhan

Today's agentic systems are typically designed, built, and deployed by single developers with full control over all participating agents. While this simplifies development, it limits the potential scale of agentic systems. We study multi-developer agentic systems (MDAs) which enable developers to collaboratively build agentic systems to push frontier capability beyond what any individual developer could achieve alone. We construct empirical and theoretical models of developers to compare MDAs with single-developer agentic systems (SDAs). On GAIA, a general assistant benchmark, a population of specialized developers whose individual agents do not exceed 35% accuracy reaches 70% when their agents are combined in an MDA. This MDA performs 12 percentage points above the median general-purpose SDA built with several times each specialist's development budget. Our theoretical models show how MDAs can incentivize specialization and encourage more efficient use of aggregated developer resources. Put together, our results make a preliminary case for MDAs as a new scaling axis by enabling developers with diverse capabilities and resources to advance agentic AI collaboratively.

##### **VASPO: Self-Distillation with Privileged Value Estimation**
2610.20516v1 by Fei Wu, Zhenrong Zhang, Jianshu Zhang, Jun Du

On-policy self-distillation provides dense supervision for reasoning models via a privileged self-teacher. However, this supervision reflects local token preferences rather than the expected return of an unguided student continuation. Recent methods combine this signal with GRPO. They use GRPO for update directions and the teacher for signal scaling. However, we demonstrate a more effective integration within PPO. Specifically, we identify complementary positional dynamics: privileged information enhances a critic's value-prediction accuracy toward the response's end, whereas self-teacher guidance is strong initially, drops mid-generation, and rebounds finally. These insights motivate Value-Augmented Self-Distillation Policy Optimization (VASPO), wherein a self-teacher and critic evaluate identical privileged information at student-generated prefixes. The critic computes token-level PPO advantages from predicted continuation returns, while the self-teacher modulates selected positive advantages via bounded probability ratios. Across six mathematical reasoning benchmarks, VASPO improves mean accuracy over PPO by 2.59 and 1.81 percentage points on Qwen3-4B and OLMo3-7B variants, respectively, and achieves the highest mean accuracy among evaluated methods on Knights and Knaves. Our code is available at https://github.com/cii030/VASPO.

摘要：

##### **ReaDy: Recurrent States as Dynamic Context for Speculative Drafting in Hybrid Linear Attention Large Language Models**
2610.20503v1 by Bohan Sun, Zunhai Su, Xialie Zhuang, Jianjin Li, Ngai Wong, Chuan-Wei Kuo

Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive generation by drafting multiple tokens per step, yet feature-based drafters rely on target hidden features whose contextual emphasis may not align with the needs of subsequent draft positions. Hybrid linear attention (HLA) models maintain compact recurrent states that accumulate historical associations, offering an efficient source of complementary context for speculative drafting. We introduce ReaDy, the first speculative decoding method, to the best of our knowledge, to leverage HLA recurrent states for position-specific context retrieval during drafting. ReaDy equips each draft position with a learned query to retrieve supplementary context directly from the target's committed recurrent states. A lightweight state reader integrates this context into draft representations through residual updates, while low-rank state reads and fused operators keep additional computation and I/O overhead low. Building on DSpark's parallel backbone and sequential prediction head, ReaDy employs a lightweight two-stage adaptation strategy, with reader capacity, state sources, and residual placement examined through ablation studies. Across seven benchmarks spanning mathematics, code generation, and dialogue, ReaDy achieves an average $3.16\times$ decoding speedup over autoregressive decoding with Qwen3.8-27B on a single NVIDIA B200 GPU. It outperforms DSpark on every benchmark, improving throughput by 7.97% and acceptance length by 8.50% on average. Our code is available at https://github.com/StartLuxLabs/ReaDy.

##### **Hindsight-Privileged RLVR: Single-Rollout Token Credit Without Group Sampling**
2610.20497v1 by Wei Chen, Yubing Wu, Zhou Xu, Haoyue Zhang, Delu Zeng, Jiahao Wang, Junmei Yang, Pan tiewen, Jingfeng Liu, Zhiheng Zhou, Bo Li, Min Chen

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) trains language models from checked task outcomes, but assigning a terminal reward to individual reasoning tokens or actions remains difficult. Group-relative methods such as Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) sample several attempts per prompt, but lose their reward-based learning signal when all attempts receive the same outcome. We propose Hindsight-Privileged RLVR (HP-RLVR), a single-rollout method that turns a completed trajectory's own future into token-level update weights. The generating policy snapshot scores each eligible token under its causal history and under a hindsight context containing the realized future and verifier feedback. A normalized Jensen--Shannon divergence between the two views weights the reward relative to a historical baseline, yielding bounded token credit for both successful and failed attempts, with no reference solution or learned critic. In paired Qwen3-8B runs over a shared $100$-step schedule, HP-RLVR trains $1.21\times$, $1.46\times$, and $2.30\times$ faster than GRPO on mathematics, code generation, and ALFWorld, using one eighth the rollouts per prompt. Accuracy is comparable overall, with HP-RLVR ahead on mathematics at all three sizes and GRPO ahead on code and ALFWorld. In ALFWorld, $43.2\%$ (4B) and $49.0\%$ (8B) of GRPO's gradient-norm records are zero, versus none for HP-RLVR.

##### **Quantifying privacy risks in foundation model representations of brain MRI**
2610.20494v1 by Imen Bajar, Héber Hwang Arcolezi, Antoine Boutet, Carole Frindel, Mohamed Maouche

Pretrained representations are increasingly used to share and reuse information from medical images without exposing the original scans, but it is unclear how much patient-specific information these representations retain. We investigate this question for brain magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) by evaluating five frozen pretrained encoders spanning different architectures, pretraining objectives, and training domains on two longitudinal T1-weighted datasets, OASIS-2 and MIRIAD. Under an attacker model with access to released embeddings, the corresponding public encoder, and auxiliary MRI scans, we evaluate patient linkage and sensitive-attribute inference. Across the evaluated representations, we observe substantial subject-specific information: rank-1 patient linkage reaches up to 99\% on MIRIAD and exceeds 90\% on OASIS-2 for the highest-risk representation, despite the absence of an identification objective during encoder training. We further show that the released embeddings retain information about a demographic attribute, with recorded-sex prediction reaching balanced accuracy of 90.9\% on OASIS-2 and 95.5\% on MIRIAD. Overall, our results demonstrate that replacing raw brain MRI with learned embeddings does not, by itself, eliminate patient-linkage or attribute-inference risk, and is therefore insufficient on its own to establish anonymity.

##### **Lost in Translation: Why Lower Bias Scores Don't Mean Fairer Models**
2610.20479v1 by Harsh Kumar

Bias audits of multilingual language models usually compare a model's average stereotype score in English with its average in another language and read the difference as a difference in fairness. We ask how much of that difference is noise. Auditing ten open models in eight languages, we measure how far scores move when English test items are merely reworded, test every language shift item by item, and check that the test catches shifts we inject on purpose. In all 70 model-language pairs the stereotype score falls below its English level, and for Hindi, Korean and Swahili the drop clearly exceeds what rewording English produces; most of it comes from StereoSet items. Judging every translation, we find that imperfect ones drop more, but faithful translations alone drop nearly as much. Still, a lower average does not show that a model is fairer. An item's English score predicts its translated score far less well than its reworded score (correlation 0.23, 0.27 on faithful translations, vs. 0.48), while scores stay as far from neutral, models agree on translated items, and within Hindi or Spanish a translated item's score correlates with its own rewording at 0.84 and 0.82. The average cannot distinguish lower bias from a different pattern of item-level scores. We also document three common pipeline errors that silently erase bias signal, and release our code.

##### **AdaRSI: On-Demand Recursive Self-Improvement for Embodied Agents**
2610.20474v1 by Peidong Wang, Ying Chang, Zhiming Ma, Xiaocui Yang, Shi Feng, Daqi Liu, Yuzhe Yang, Xu Cao, Chuang Wang, Jian Su, Daling Wang

Pretrained vision-language-action models (VLAs) often fail midway through unseen long-horizon tasks. Recursive self-improvement (RSI) offers a remedy, but embodied RSI methods either refine the harness around a frozen executor and so cannot add a missing motion, or train the executor on persistent failures without asking whether a failure lies in how the executor is called or in what it can do. We ask when a self-improving embodied system should change the weights of its executor, and propose AdaRSI, an on-demand RSI framework for embodied agents. A coding agent first improves its skills and harness, then attributes persistent failures by retrying failing sub-steps from saved states, and fine-tunes only the sub-steps that remain unreliable, with demonstrations it collects itself and experience carried over from earlier tasks. On all 50 target tasks of RoboCasa365, with GR00T N1.5 as the executor and no human demonstrations, AdaRSI improves the VLA on all three suites and raises its success on unseen composite tasks from 4.6% to 13.8%. Compared with fine-tuning every sub-step, it reaches slightly higher success while using only 28% of the GPU time and 22% of the self-collected demonstrations, and fine-tuning without a harness trains more yet gains far less.

##### **SafeAdapt-Poker: Counterfactual Risk Calibration for Budgeted Adaptation under Opponent Shift**
2610.20473v1 by Miaobo Hu, Shuhao Hu, Xiaobo Guo, Xin Wang, Bokun Wang, Rui Chen, Daren Zha, Jun Xiao

Opponent histories can mislead after behavioral shifts. SafeAdapt-Poker formulates budgeted adaptation under opponent shift as a dual-counterfactual sequential-control problem using only information available before each decision. At each decision the controller asks two distinct questions: what local downside a deviation from the frozen anchor would induce under matched legal continuations, and what future adaptation value would be lost by consuming part of the remaining deviation budget now. The first question is answered by a monotone strategic-risk model over deviation intensity; the second by a paired budget-state continuation model that prices current budget consumption. The controller selects among support-preserving candidate deviations by trading immediate adaptation value against these two quantities, a pathwise cumulative-KL ledger bounds total anchor-relative deviation, and a finite family of frozen controllers is screened on complete paired calibration sessions before held-out interaction. A finite-candidate perturbation bound shows that the allocation objective degrades continuously with continuation-value estimation error. Evaluation covers exact-game exploitation and safety, four opponent shifts, local-risk calibration, delayed-opportunity allocation, direct opportunity-value estimation, value-error robustness, factorial mechanism attribution, budget sensitivity, abstract-HUNL cross-play, and player/time-held-out public-history behavior. On exact Leduc the full controller attains a matched weak-pool gain of +0.0718 with a worst-opponent delta of -0.0126, raises delayed-opportunity capture from 0.6318 under myopic allocation to 0.7841 under the same session budget, and reaches +42.7 mbb/hand in abstract-HUNL cross-play; the retained public-history screen reports 0.5820 action accuracy and 0.9277 log loss versus 0.5039 and 0.9778 for a public prior.

##### **ChronoCulture: Evaluating Cultural Awareness Across Time in Large Language Models**
2610.20472v1 by Rusali Saha, Nitya Khurana, Ruihong Huang, Kuan-Hao Huang

Cultural attitudes and social norms evolve over time, yet most cultural benchmarks for large language models (LLMs) treat culture as a static attribute of a country or region. We introduce ChronoCulture, a benchmark of 10,360 temporal pairs built from longitudinal survey data across 72 countries. Each pair contains the same survey question asked in the same country in an older and a newer year, with full population response distributions as ground truth. Across six LLMs, we find that models can often predict a country's attitudes at one point in time but do not reliably capture how those attitudes change. Their predictions for different years tend to stay close together, failing to capture much of the observed change. Specifying the intended year in the prompt also offers little overall improvement in alignment with survey responses. These findings suggest that models often present a largely static picture of cultural attitudes, highlighting the need to evaluate cultural awareness across time.

##### **Equal CER, Unequal Damage: Severity-Weighted Character Error Rate for OCR**
2610.20461v1 by Nguyen Nguyen, Yongyi Zang

Character Error Rate (CER), the standard metric for OCR and vision-language models (VLMs), charges a typo as much as a wrong name. We introduce semCER, which keeps CER's alignment and denominator and charges each grapheme edit a severity from an LLM judge, with no word segmentation. Normalized CER keeps the score of a whole transcription, where it tracks readers as well as semCER or better, and semCER breaks that score down into named edits. The charges track readers' ratings edit by edit in English, Vietnamese, and Chinese and put the edits readers rate most severe near the top of a sorted list. On pairs drawn where CER and semCER disagreed, readers side with semCER more often than with normalized CER in English and Chinese, and equally often in Vietnamese. Across 16 languages, the judge charges a wrong diacritic or tone mark almost nothing in Spanish but nearly a wrong letter's severity in Telugu. Where we can count real words, a general VLM's wrong letter forms one far above chance while a classical pipeline's stays near it. We release the 61 million judged edits, the aggregated reader ratings, and the distilled scorer at https://github.com/nguyenx2t/semcer.

##### **MolLean: A Process-Verifiable Language for Chemical Reasoning**
2610.20459v1 by Jiatong Li, Yatao Bian

Chemical chain-of-thought can contain hallucinated structural and property claims. We thereby introduce MolLean, a process-verifiable language that grounds chemical reasoning in an explicit molecular state. A layered Lean-based kernel checks state transitions and assertions over molecular readouts, from primitive graph edits to compound transformations. Meanwhile, a typed witness protocol separates native structural checks from externally supplied measurements. We further introduce MolLean-MCP, a stateful tool interface that exposes these transitions, checks, and readouts during generation: the model receives feedback to decide its next action, and rejected actions leave the state unchanged. MolLean supports one-pass trace execution, whole-trace refinement, and interactive reasoning through MolLean-MCP. We evaluate these three modes with direct chain-of-thought on 8,800 matched examples across 12 tasks spanning molecular generation, editing, optimization, and reactions. With Qwen3.6-27B, whole-trace refinement outperforms direct chain-of-thought on all 12 tasks. MolLean-MCP complements whole-trace refinement with stepwise verification feedback, reaching better performance in reaction tasks. Together, these results highlight an executable foundation for chemical reasoning, in which explicit chemical commitments can be checked and revised rather than remaining solely in free-form explanations.

##### **Same Claim, Different Interpretations: How LLM Agents Resolve Ambiguity in Scientific Claims**
2610.20457v1 by Delip Rao, Ahmed Muharram, Chris Callison-Burch

Co-scientists and agentic scientific claim verifiers are increasingly used to assess claims across the sciences. Natural-language scientific claims, written for human experts, are often underspecified and so admit several defensible interpretations. Such verifiers usually pick one, often tacitly, and benchmarks score their verdicts against expert labels without recording the expert's interpretation or the verifier's in a comparable form. This leads to disagreements with expert labels that cannot be traced to the evidence or the interpretation, and to compute spent on unchecked interpretations. We introduce Claim Cards to make such interpretations explicit in the reasoning itself. A Claim Card records a verifier's interpretation as nine fields before evidence gathering, each marked stated, inferred, or default. To test whether an agent's interpretation can change its verdict, we ran a GPT-5.4 agent five times on each of 60 expert-labeled materials-science and biomedical claims with a card, without one, and with one card frozen across runs (900 runs). Over 40% of card fields were not marked stated, and a card added about a tenth to a run's GPT-5.4 tokens and time without measurably changing agreement with expert labels. The agent's own interpretations varied between runs, although within a claim this variation did not measurably track the verdict. A deliberate change to one field of the frozen card, however, making the quantifier universal or the threshold for support stricter, changed the verdict on about a third of the claims, against about one in seven expected by chance (p < 0.001 for each edit). On 24 materials-science claims run with computational tools, the stricter threshold also changed what the agent asked those tools to compute. We therefore argue that verdicts should be reported with their interpretations and that benchmarks could publish the interpretation behind each label.

##### **The COUNT: Counting N-grams Unlocks Scaling on Host Memory and Training-Free Adaptation**
2610.20456v1 by Ian Magnusson, Noah A. Smith, Pang Wei Koh

The scaling relationship of compute and performance for language models (LMs) has been extensively studied; more recently, models are being designed to scale memory use, as well. In particular, adding embeddings for n-grams scales memory separately from compute use, because each pass only processes individual embedding rows, which are much smaller than the storage used for the whole embedding table. However, existing methods train embeddings from scratch, which needs expensive GPU memory for fast reading and writing. Meanwhile, the ordinary memory of the host system is cheap and underutilized by these training methods. To scale on host memory, we introduce COUNT (Counting Offline Unlocks N-gram Transformers), which builds n-gram embeddings without adding learned parameters by storing precomputed n-gram counts on host. We use the corpus frequencies to weight together the LM's own unembeddings of the next tokens most often following the current n-gram history. At a fixed compute budget, COUNT can be added as an additional input embedding for pretraining 1B-parameter LMs to improve validation loss as much as an LM with 57% more compute. If GPU memory is limited, using COUNT to let backbone parameters fill GPU memory gives better performance than splitting memory with additional learned n-gram embeddings. Beyond efficiency, COUNT allows switching in a different table of n-gram counts from a domain-specific corpus at inference time to adapt to a new task without any training.

##### **From Training to Escalation: Forensic Analysis and Local Recreation of RL/RLHF-Induced Privilege Escalation in AI Agents (CVE-2026-65616)**
2610.20437v1 by Alicia Colmenero-Fernández

Mid-2026 reporting on autonomous AI evaluation incidents claimed that frontier large language model (LLM) agents escaped server containment through emergent coordination and cloud infrastructure attacks. This paper contextualizes those claims through forensic analysis and containerized recreation of both the Highly Persistent Internal Model (HPIM) training-case conditions and the Phase One privilege escalation targeting internal package repositories during OpenAI's ExploitGym evaluations. We recreate the configuration of the OpenAI-provided training example and reproduce the cryptographic verification bypass (CVE-2026-65616) in isolated repository servers, where misconfigured systems enabled token forgery and privilege escalation through basic HTTP requests, Python, and JavaScript. Crucially, the training-example conditions and the Phase One (and later) misconfigurations share the same exploitable patterns. Our analysis explains how Zero Trust Architecture principles neutralize this shared attack vector. Contemporary AI risks derive directly from training and deployment conditions in ambivalent learning environments lacking adequate containment measures.

2610.20419v1 by Fazle Mohammed Tasfiq, Sabab Attin, Sheikh Md. Tahmid Hossain, Raiat Reza, Tanzim Ahmmed, Nafis Chowdhury, Anika Ahmed, Moinul Haque, Farig Yousuf Sadeque

Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) models have become almost a cornerstone of open-domain question answering tasks; in which the quality of answers generated in the process, greatly relies on the effectiveness of retrieval mechanisms. In the realm of LLM, the last two years have seen tremendous progress in RAG architectures, with many models reporting to have improved performance through various design innovations. The cultural question and answering systems of South Asia, in particular, present a specific challenge since the region is home to the vast number of languages and traditions, which are closely interrelated. ##### **OrionMoE-v1.0: A Mixture-of-Experts Foundation Model for Tabular In-Context Learning**
2610.20418v1 by Mohamed Bouadi, Nassim Bouarour, Aditya Tanna, Vinay Kumar Sankarapu

Tabular foundation models enable in-context learning by predicting unseen observations from a labeled support table without task-specific parameter updates. Despite recent advances, most existing approaches rely on dense architectures that apply the same transformations across features and datasets. We introduce OrionMoE-Small, a compact tabular foundation model that combines distribution-aware representations with dataset-conditioned sparse mixture-of-experts computation. Built on a dual-axis Transformer architecture, OrionMoE-Small selectively activates experts along the feature axis, with routing decisions informed by both individual feature representations and the broader dataset context. This design enables adaptive allocation of computation across heterogeneous tabular problems while retaining a shared computational pathway across tasks. OrionMoE-Small is pretrained entirely on synthetic classification tasks generated from a structured causal prior and performs downstream prediction without gradient-based adaptation. Experiments across multiple tabular benchmarks demonstrate competitive performance against substantially larger foundation models, with particularly strong results on small-context and high-dimensional datasets. Further analysis reveals favorable relative performance under class imbalance and highlights the importance of feature composition.

##### **From Unreliable to Usable: Decomposing Failure Modes and Confidence Signals in LLM Entity Extraction - Evidence from Cyber Threat Intelligence**
2610.20417v1 by Jiahong Qian

Audits of large language model (LLM) extraction from full-length documents report severe unreliability, leaving two questions open: what the failures actually consist of, and whether confidence signals can turn unreliable output into usable output. We run a preregistered, fully frozen evaluation of two new-generation low-cost LLMs (DeepSeek-V4-Flash, GLM-5.3-Flash) with a flagship-tier control (GPT-6.1-Sol) on a public corpus of 354 incident campaigns (315 unique reports, 630 model-report evaluations), scoring extraction quality against gold, confidence calibration, and robustness to single-sentence perturbations. Three findings emerge. (1) Unreliability persists across tiers: micro precision is 0.03-0.34 and verbalized calibration error (ECE) 0.53-0.94 across both splits; the gap does not close at the flagship tier. (2) The dominant failure is not hallucination: a full mention census plus a three-tier annotation of 360 sampled candidates (two independent LLM annotators from vendors outside the evaluated set, Cohen's kappa = 0.79 on the preregistered acceptance line, fully disclosed) back-projects to 65.6% background mentions and 19.9% gold-scope gaps, with only 1.2% unsupported candidates. (3) Confidence ranks but does not verify: zero-cost self-consistency-frequency tiering passes a preregistered top-30% precision-gain bar in all six test-split model-type cells (4/6 on train), the zero-cost text-frequency x SC frequency combination wins 3, ties 2, loses 1 across cells, yet relation-level counterfactuals on all 45 targets show that a single misattribution sentence flips high-confidence judgments in 95.6-100% of cases, and even a background sentence explicitly marked as unrelated still binds a fake vulnerability in up to 26.7% of them. ##### **When Does Momentum Help in Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards?**
2610.20413v1 by Kaifei Wang, Zikang Shan, Liwei Wang, Yinyu Ye, Han Zhong

Momentum is a classical tool for accelerating convex optimization and a standard component of modern large language model training. Yet recent empirical work has questioned the usefulness of first-moment momentum in reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). We study this question through a bandit formulation of RLVR with bias-corrected exponential moving average (EMA) momentum, where the EMA coefficient $β$ controls how quickly the weights on past gradients decay. When policy gradients are evaluated exactly, we prove that increasing $β$ within a range that prevents overshoot strictly accelerates optimization. When gradients are estimated from sampled responses, a positive expected gain can be obscured by sampling fluctuations. We show that as the learning rate $η$ decreases, the expected gain with fixed $β$ vanishes faster than these fluctuations, whereas increasing $β$ to keep $η/(1-β)$ within the safe regime preserves a nonvanishing gain that eventually dominates them. Bandit simulations quantitatively validate these predictions. RLVR experiments on Qwen3-4B-Base using SGD with EMA and AdamW support our principle of choosing larger coefficients in practical LLM post-training. On Qwen2.5-32B with AdamW, simply increasing $β_1$ improves mean validation accuracy by nearly $2$ percentage points over the conventional choice, demonstrating the principle's effectiveness at a larger model scale.

##### **Cortex: Consolidating Context into Parametric Memory**
2610.20399v1 by Jintian Zhang, Feng He, Xinnian Liang, Ziwen Xu, Haoming Xu, Haojing Huang, Shuangzhi Wu, Ningyu Zhang

Context can improve large language model performance, but these gains may be lost after context removal. We introduce a five-task-family testbed measuring consolidation and retention under single, batch, and sequential updates. We propose Cortex, which generates prospective queries to anticipate uses of context and distills context-conditioned outputs into mergeable low-rank or structural feed-forward network updates. ##### **FOLD: Position-Free Far Memory with Local Order for Long-Context Speculative Decoding**
2610.20392v1 by Shuxuan Li, Jialiang Kang, Yanghao Zhou, Ziye Zhu, Xiao Yang

Speculative decoding accelerates LLM generation through lightweight drafting and parallel verification. It is particularly promising for long inputs, where KV-cache access costs can be amortized over multiple accepted tokens. Yet most drafters are trained on short sequences and degrade on long inputs, while long-context training is costly. Existing training-free approaches restrict the context window, rescale positions, or augment recent context with retrieved distant entries. Drafters, however, read target hidden states that already encode contextual and positional information, and their lightweight architecture makes full-history attention affordable. We propose FOLD, a training-free method that preserves exact local positions and treats all distant entries as far memory at a shared position. To counter attention dilution as the memory grows, we sharpen attention within it to emphasize relevant entries while separately controlling its total weight relative to the local window. Across six target-drafter pairs and diverse benchmarks of up to 256K tokens, FOLD predominantly delivers the highest speedup, with the best case more than doubling the speedup over autoregressive decoding, from 2.08$\times$ to 4.22$\times$.

2610.20388v1 by Xiaoxuan Dong, Jin Zhang, Zichen Yuan, Yue Zhou, Zihao Rao, Dexu Yu, Tingyong WU, Wenhao Deng, Junchen Fu, Pengwei Sun, Yang Ran, Bo Lan, Yongxin Ni, Youhua Li

##### **Cascaded Retrieval with Cross-Modal Query Refinement for Efficient Visual Document Search**
2610.20367v1 by Diana Turmakhan, Aseem Srivastava, Vivek Gupta, Iryna Gurevych, Thamar Solorio

Retrieving information from visually rich documents requires matching queries against pages that contain complex evidence in tables, figures, and layout-dependent structures. Current vision-language retrievers address this but incur query-time costs that grow with corpus size and are impractical to scale. We propose SCOUT, a cascaded two-track retrieval framework that separates retrieval performance from exhaustive search. The visual track first uses a lightweight parent retriever to filter the corpus to a compact candidate pool, then applies a stronger multi-vector reranker only to those candidates. In parallel, a semantic track retrieves over hierarchical page and document summaries generated offline to provide a signal complementary to visual matching. The outputs are fused through cross-modal query refinement, which uses semantic scores to adjust the visual query representation before final ranking. We evaluate SCOUT on 4 major benchmarks against 6 baselines and observe a significant query-time latency improvement up to 80.7x (28.8x on average) relative to the best baseline, while exceeding state-of-the-art retrieval performance by up to 7.8% NDCG across all benchmarks.

##### **Human Demonstrations as Post-Training Data for Any Robot Body: Retargeting the Object, Not the Hand**
2610.20351v1 by Nagasrikanth Kallakuri

Human demonstrations are today used largely for pretraining vision-language-action models, with little ability to use the same data for target-embodiment or task-oriented training, which scalable deployment of robots in industrial and general environments depends on. A human demonstration recorded on a simple and scalable device such as a mobile phone can serve as post-training data for a robot whose body differs in structure and capability from the human who recorded it. This is made possible not by retargeting the hand's motion but the object's. A capture yields a body-free record: the object's pose over time, its contact intervals, and a reconstructed 3D object with provenance. Any robot, given as a URDF and a short descriptor, is solved against that record and scored in simulation against the demonstrated object motion as ground truth: the human-to-simulation leg of human-to-simulation-to-robot transfer, measured per robot. Each (robot, episode) pair gets one outcome: pass, failure with a named mode, or refusal, with its reason, before any motion. The method runs as a working system over a growing collection; the set analysed here is a sample of it: 42 captures solved on 8 robots, 157 pairs, of which 31.2% pass, 52.2% fail, 14.0% are refused and 2.5% are unauditable (kinematic feasibility, +/-50 mm / +/-15 deg, in simulation). Refusals held up: every pair the checks refuse on stored evidence yet ran anyway failed (13 of 13; 5 on a body whose public model welds its hand). A description file could not be shown to screen a robot: its outcomes were barely more predictable than guessing. No policy has yet been trained on the record. The record decides what a robot may post-train on; whether the policy then works on the real robot is the simulation-to-robot question, answered downstream.

##### **Doc2Harness: Benchmarking LLMs on Long-Horizon Harness Engineering of Coding Agents**
2610.20344v1 by Yuyao Ge, Shenghua Liu, Peizhong Gao, Chendi Ge, Chenxu Liu, Yiwei Wang, Lingrui Mei, Baolong Bi, Jiayu Yao, Jiafeng Guo, Xueqi Cheng

Agents driven by large language models are increasingly deployed on long-horizon tasks, where they use tools and modify real workspaces. How well they perform depends as much on the harness around the model as on the model itself. Existing benchmarks measure downstream task success under a fixed harness, leaving harness engineering itself unexplored. We propose Doc2Harness, a two-stage benchmark that evaluates long-horizon harness engineering directly. In Stage 1, a build model operating inside a fixed builder harness receives an interface-only contract skeleton and a development document that consolidates behavior across twelve frontier harnesses, and then constructs a complete candidate harness in an isolated container. In Stage 2, the frozen candidate harness runs in containers separate from the construction environment, one per test case, and is driven end to end by a scripted mock model. We score each test case using four observable channels, namely the requests sent to the model, the files on disk, the process exit code, and the command output; positive controls rule out vacuous passes. Across nineteen frontier models, each evaluated with three independent builds on 328 test cases, GPT-6 Astra achieves the highest pass rate at 53.05%, while the weakest model reaches 27.44%, a gap of roughly twenty-six percentage points. Even the strongest model fails nearly half of the test cases, and failures cluster around how cross-cutting functionality is split across modules. To our knowledge, Doc2Harness provides the first deterministic benchmark for measuring long-horizon harness engineering.

##### **Read, Parse, Describe: Unified Document Parsing with Visual Element Description Generation**

This separation leaves scientific figures, captions, and visual semantics weakly connected, and fails to measure whether a model can produce a single page-level parse that is both structurally faithful and semantically accessible. We introduce Read-Parse-Describe, a unified dataset for document parsing with visual element description generation. Given a page image, models are required to output an ordered list of layout elements containing bounding boxes, semantic labels, reading-order indices, recognized textual content, and alternative text descriptions for visual elements. The benchmark is built from 2,402 documents, comprising 18,163 continuous pages, 125,218 layout elements, and 11,338 visual instances. Besides, we further propose a plug-and-play space-awareness enhancer that reuses multi-layer visual features and injects grid-aware spatial guidance into vision-language tokens for layout-aware generation. Experiments across end-to-end VLMs and pipeline parsers show that existing systems still struggle to jointly recover reading order and visual semantics, while our enhanced model achieves strong joint performance. These results highlight the need to move beyond isolated document parsing modules toward unified models that jointly read, parse, and describe complex documents. The new dataset and code will be made publicly available at https://github.com/yufanchen96/RPD

##### **TreeFlex: Agents Navigating Their Own Search Trees**
2610.20334v1 by Dongzhuo Li, Fangzhou Cheng, Weiwei Li, Kevin Han, Haixu Ma, Zhongchang Sun, Ruiyi Li, Serena Li, Xiangjun Fan, Lizhu Zhang, Yuandong Tian

A ReAct-style agent accumulates observations, intermediate results, and failed attempts in a single context. As this context grows, stale and off-path information consumes its limited capacity and obscures relevant evidence, making focused reasoning increasingly difficult. We introduce TreeFlex, a framework in which the agent's own policy decides where to search. The agent records each intermediate state, with a short summary, as a node in a search tree maintained by the harness. The policy is a single language model that acts in two roles. In the Reflection role, it has a global view of the tree through these summaries and picks a node to resume, together with an instruction. In the Execution role, the harness restores the path that led to that node, and the model carries out the instruction with the usual task tools until it saves a new node or proposes a solution, which Reflection then approves or sends back to another node. Through these choices, the policy decides whether to deepen the current line, branch from or revisit an earlier state, verify a proposal, or stop, without a fixed traversal order or a score for each node. We train navigation, task actions, checkpointing, and proposal review jointly with a tree-aware adaptation of GRPO, using task-level outcome rewards augmented by process rewards. After small-scale RL of a 4B model, with the same active-context limit for all methods, TreeFlex reaches 48.8% accuracy on a challenging deep-research benchmark, versus 41.5% for flat ReAct and 42.8% for FoldAgent, and its Pass@8 is about ten points higher than either. On SWE-bench Verified, with all three systems trained with RL from the same 9B model, TreeFlex resolves 54.9% of tasks, versus 43.3% for FoldAgent and 45.2% for its step-wise variant.

##### **Argument Graph Uncertainty: Quantifying Uncertainty from the Logical Structure of Reasoning Chains**
2610.20328v1 by Simon Baur, Sumire Honda, Wojciech Samek, Jackie Ma

Reliable uncertainty estimation is essential for deploying large language models in high-stakes reasoning tasks, yet most existing approaches rely on expensive sampling strategies or shallow token-level signals that ignore the internal logical structure of model outputs. We introduce Argument Graph Uncertainty, a post-hoc framework for multiple choice question tasks, that estimates model confidence directly from the logical structure of a single chain-of-thought reasoning trace. Our method uses either a lightweight instruction-tuned model or a simple deterministic rule to segment the reasoning chain into propositional units, then applies a NLI model to infer relational edges and construct two complementary argument graphs over these segments. The resulting edge scores are aggregated into a Dirichlet posterior over answer options, whose concentration yields calibrated uncertainty estimates reflecting both local step-to-step coherence and global logical consistency across the reasoning trace. Evaluated on GPQA, MMLU-Pro and TruthfulQA, our method consistently surpasses token-based and consistency-based uncertainty baselines across multiple reasoning models. We further demonstrate through extensive combination analyses that argument graph uncertainty captures an orthogonal signal to existing approaches, making it a complementary component in uncertainty ensembles. Crucially, our framework requires no additional calls to the evaluated model and relies entirely on small, efficient auxiliary models, producing fully interpretable, per-node uncertainty attributions at low computational cost.

##### **Anuvani-Diarization: Zero Shot Speaker Diarization and Annotation Conventions Across 22 Languages**

India's 22 scheduled languages are spoken by over a billion people in conversations that mix languages, often within one turn, overlap freely, and take place over telephones, in meetings, and in media. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly deployed in safety-critical domains, yet their susceptibility to backdoor attacks remains a formidable challenge. While existing defenses have evolved from visual-centric patch cues to unsupervised internal attention statistics, they are fundamentally constrained by a reliance on static snapshots and attack-specific heuristics. Given the intricate interplay between dual modalities, the vast design space for multimodal triggers allows diverse threats, such as textual injections, sub-perceptual noises, and coordinated cross-modal triggers, to easily circumvent such rigid priors, particularly when attack types are unknown in real-world applications. In this paper, we rethink backdoor detection by shifting the diagnostic paradigm from static observations to dynamic output evolution. To capture this behavior, we propose EVO-Detect, a unified detection framework that monitors the dynamic evolution of internal signals during early decoding steps through two complementary channels: Attention Flow and Logits Flow. The attention branch measures how image-text attention allocation evolves during early generation, whereas the logits branch quantifies the sensitivity of the output distribution to modal perturbations through interventional ablations. ##### **CASI: Credit Assignment via Set Intervention for Agentic Retrieval**
2610.20293v1 by Xinyi Cheng, Tianfu Zhang, Jiwen Li, Xuesen Zhang, Shihao Yu, Pengfei Zhu,  Renhuan, Heda Wang

##### **FUSE: Fairness-aware Update Screening against Byzantine Exploitation in $q$-Fair Federated Learning**
2610.20264v1 by Tiantong Sun, Jinlin Liu, Xingru Wei, Zequan Fang, Wei Chen, Qiang Yang, Xiaojin Zhang

Client-level fair federated learning, in the form of $q$-fair federated learning ($q$-FFL), agnostic federated learning, or tilted empirical risk minimization, has emerged as an attractive solution to equalize utility across heterogeneous clients. This paradigm grants high-loss clients greater relative influence, while a central server aggregates their loss-weighted updates into a single shared model.
